        Idea:
# Local-worker HTTP endpoints + file:// input handling

This task lives in the **reigh-worker** repo (a Python project that polls a Supabase queue for tasks and executes them on GPU). It's the worker side of the local-file-handle work that already shipped on the reigh-app side.

## Background (why this exists)

reigh-app now has two flows for getting a user's local file (a `FileSystemFileHandle` persisted in the browser) into a worker task as input:

- **Remote worker fallback (already shipped, used today)**: app uploads the bytes to Supabase Storage, puts a public URL into task params, worker downloads from the URL.
- **Local worker fast path (DORMANT — what this task enables)**: when the worker is running locally on the same machine as the user's browser, the resolver in `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/{healthcheck,ingest}.ts` is supposed to:
  1. probe `http://localhost:<port>/health` to detect the worker
  2. POST file bytes to `http://localhost:<port>/ingest`, receive back `{ path: "/abs/path/on/disk" }`
  3. put `file://<path>` into task params
  4. after task completion, POST to `http://localhost:<port>/cleanup` with the path so the worker deletes the materialized copy

The worker today has no HTTP server — it's a polling client (`source/runtime/worker/server.py` despite its name is not an HTTP server). It also can't read `file://` URLs in most code paths (see #2 below).

This task is what makes the local fast-path actually work end-to-end.

## What to build

### 1. HTTP server with three endpoints in reigh-worker

Add a small HTTP server that runs alongside the existing polling worker process. Endpoints:

- `GET /health` — returns 200 + JSON `{"ok": true, "worker_id": "...", "version": "..."}`. Cheap, no auth check beyond localhost.
- `POST /ingest` — accepts `multipart/form-data` with one file part. Writes the bytes to a known materialization dir (e.g. `~/.reigh-local-files/<uuid>.<ext>`), returns 200 + `{"path": "/abs/path", "size": <bytes>}`. Auth: see below.
- `POST /cleanup` — accepts JSON `{"path": "/abs/path"}`. Validates the path is inside the materialization dir (no escapes), deletes it, returns 204. Auth: see below.

**Auth model**: bind to `127.0.0.1` only (NOT `0.0.0.0`). Even on localhost, an attacker process on the same machine could hit the endpoints — so require a shared secret on `/ingest` and `/cleanup` via `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header. The token is generated at worker startup, written to a 0600 file at `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (owner-only readable). The reigh-app side reads that file before making requests.

`/health` does NOT require the token (otherwise the probe can't tell "worker present" from "wrong token"); it leaks zero sensitive info.

**Server framework**: pick whichever is least invasive — stdlib `http.server` is fine for this scale (<10 req/sec, single user). Avoid pulling in FastAPI/aiohttp unless they're already present in worker deps. Investigate `pyproject.toml` / `requirements.txt` first.

**Lifecycle**: server runs in a background thread spun up at worker startup, dies cleanly on shutdown (SIGINT/SIGTERM). It's PAT-mode-only — when the worker is launched with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (cloud mode), do NOT start the server. Detection logic exists in `source/runtime/worker/server.py:_resolve_worker_db_client_key()` — reuse it, don't duplicate.

**Port**: configurable via env var (e.g. `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT`), default to a sensible high port. Write the chosen port to `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` so reigh-app can find it (alongside the auth-token file).

**Materialization dir**: configurable env var (e.g. `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR`), default to `~/.reigh-local-files`. Create with mode 0700 if missing. Hard cap: never write outside this dir; never accept a path component containing `..`.

### 2. file:// support in download_utils.py

`source/utils/download_utils.py` is what the worker uses to fetch task input bytes. Currently it handles `http://` and `https://` URLs by streaming with `requests.get()`. For `file://` URLs (which the new local fast-path produces), it does NOT strip the scheme — it treats `file:///path/to/foo.jpg` as a literal local path, which fails in most call sites.

Fix: in the URL-detection helper (currently around line 205–206), recognize `file://` as a URL scheme. When the scheme is `file`, strip the scheme prefix to get the local path, validate that the path exists and is inside an allowed dir (see materialization dir above — only allow file:// inputs that point inside that dir), then return the path WITHOUT downloading. Don't copy the file; the worker is allowed to read it in place.

Apply this to BOTH `download_image_if_url()` and `download_video_if_url()` (and any siblings). There's apparently a working `file://` handler at `source/media/structure/download.py` — read it for reference but don't depend on it (the image/video paths are the high-value ones).

### 3. Wire the cleanup hook to actually fire

The reigh-app side's `complete_task` edge function calls back into the worker's `/cleanup` for each materialized input. Verify that wiring works end-to-end. If the edge function passes the materialized paths through some indirection, make sure the cleanup actually reaches the right worker.

The edge function code is in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts` (already shipped). Read it to understand the contract — what URL does it call, what payload does it send, what does it expect back?

## Tests

- `/health` returns 200, no auth.
- `/ingest` rejects without auth token (401).
- `/ingest` rejects with wrong token (401).
- `/ingest` accepts multipart upload, writes file, returns path.
- `/cleanup` rejects path-traversal (`/etc/passwd`, `../../foo`).
- `/cleanup` deletes valid file, returns 204.
- `/cleanup` is idempotent (already-deleted file → 204, not 500).
- `download_image_if_url` and `download_video_if_url` accept `file://` paths inside materialization dir.
- Both reject `file://` paths outside materialization dir (security — don't let task params point at arbitrary local files).
- Server doesn't start when `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is set (cloud mode).
- Server starts when only PAT is set (local mode).

Use the worker's existing test framework (likely pytest — check `pyproject.toml`).

## Constraints

- Don't refactor unrelated code. Don't add new abstractions. Don't add comments explaining the change.
- Reuse existing helpers — particularly the auth-mode detection in `source/runtime/worker/server.py`.
- Don't pull in heavyweight HTTP frameworks. `http.server` from stdlib is sufficient. If you genuinely need something more, justify it in the plan.
- Keep the server code in a clearly-named module (e.g. `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`).

## Out of scope

- The reigh-app side is already done — don't touch it unless you find a real bug in `localWorker/{healthcheck,ingest}.ts` that blocks the integration. Flag, don't fix.
- Don't add metrics, logging frameworks, prometheus, etc. Plain `print()` or whatever the worker already uses is fine.

## Verification

- Run the test suite (`pytest` or whatever the project uses).
- Manually trace the auth flow end-to-end: server starts → token + port written → reigh-app can read both → `/health` reachable → `/ingest` accepts an authed upload → `/cleanup` removes it.
- Confirm cloud-mode launch (with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`) does not start the server.

## Deliverables

- New module(s) for the HTTP server, with tests.
- `download_utils.py` updated for `file://` with tests.
- Lifecycle integration into worker startup/shutdown.
- A short note in your final summary describing: which port + dir defaults you chose, where the auth token + port file live on disk, any places the reigh-app side will need a small follow-up tweak.

```
47	def _resolve_worker_db_client_key(cli_args, *, access_token: str | None) -> str:
48	    auth_mode = os.environ.get("WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE", "").strip().lower()
49	    service_key = os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") or os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY")
50	    anon_key = getattr(cli_args, "supabase_anon_key", None) or os.environ.get("SUPABASE_ANON_KEY")
51	
52	    if auth_mode == "service":
53	        if not service_key:
54	            raise ValueError("SERVICE_ROLE_KEY is required when WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=service")
55	        return service_key
56	    if auth_mode == "worker":
57	        if not access_token:
58	            raise ValueError("WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=worker requires --reigh-access-token")
59	        return access_token
60	    # Default: service key > access token > anon key (matches old worker.py behavior)
61	    resolved = service_key or access_token or anon_key
62	    if not resolved:
63	        raise ValueError("No Supabase key found. Provide --reigh-access-token, SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY, or SUPABASE_ANON_KEY")
64	    return resolved

227	    parser.add_argument("--supabase-access-token", type=str, default=None, help="Legacy alias for --reigh-access-token")
228	    parser.add_argument("--supabase-anon-key", type=str, default=None, help="Supabase anon key (set via env SUPABASE_ANON_KEY)")
229	    idle_release.add_cli_args(parser)
230	
231	    # WGP Globals

233	    parser.add_argument("--wgp-compile", type=str, default=None)

238	    parser.add_argument("--wgp-transformer-dtype-policy", type=str, default=None)

242	    parser.add_argument("--wgp-preload-policy", type=str, default=None)
243	    parser.add_argument("--wgp-preload", type=int, default=None)
244	
245	    return parser.parse_args()

247	
248	def main():
249	    import sys
250	    import time
251	    import datetime
252	    import logging
253	    from dotenv import load_dotenv
254	
255	    headless_logger.essential("main() entered")
256	
257	    load_dotenv()
258	    bootstrap_runtime_environment()
259	
260	    # -----------------------------------------------------------------------
261	    # Subprocess tracker — records every subprocess we spawn so we can
262	    # report them at shutdown time for forensic analysis of phantom CTRL_C.
263	    # -----------------------------------------------------------------------
264	    import threading as _threading_mod
265	    _subprocess_registry_lock = _threading_mod.Lock()
266	    _subprocess_registry: list[dict] = []  # {pid, cmd, started, ended, returncode}
267	
268	    def _register_subprocess(pid: int, cmd: str):
269	        with _subprocess_registry_lock:
270	            _subprocess_registry.append({
271	                "pid": pid, "cmd": cmd,
272	                "started": time.time(), "ended": None, "returncode": None,
273	            })
274	
275	    def _deregister_subprocess(pid: int, returncode: int | None):
276	        with _subprocess_registry_lock:
277	            for entry in _subprocess_registry:
278	                if entry["pid"] == pid and entry["ended"] is None:
279	                    entry["ended"] = time.time()
280	                    entry["returncode"] = returncode
281	                    break
282	
283	    def _dump_subprocess_registry():
284	        """Log all tracked subprocesses — active and recently exited."""
285	        with _subprocess_registry_lock:
286	            if not _subprocess_registry:
287	                headless_logger.essential("[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] No tracked subprocesses")
288	                return
289	            now = time.time()
290	            for entry in _subprocess_registry:
291	                status = "ACTIVE" if entry["ended"] is None else f"exited rc={entry['returncode']}"
292	                age = now - entry["started"]
293	                ended_ago = f", ended {now - entry['ended']:.1f}s ago" if entry["ended"] else ""
294	                headless_logger.essential(
295	                    f"[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] subprocess pid={entry['pid']} "
296	                    f"status={status} age={age:.1f}s{ended_ago} cmd={entry['cmd'][:120]}"
297	                )
298	
299	    # Expose the registry globally so subprocess_utils can use it
300	    globals()["_register_subprocess"] = _register_subprocess
301	    globals()["_deregister_subprocess"] = _deregister_subprocess
302	
303	    def _dump_all_thread_stacks():
304	        """Log stack traces for ALL threads, not just the main thread."""
305	        import traceback as _tb
306	        headless_logger.essential("[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] --- All thread stacks ---")
307	        for thread_id, frame in sys._current_frames().items():
308	            # Try to find the thread name
309	            thread_name = "unknown"
310	            for t in _threading_mod.enumerate():
311	                if t.ident == thread_id:
312	                    thread_name = t.name
313	                    break
314	            headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] Thread {thread_id} ({thread_name}):")
315	            for line in _tb.format_stack(frame):
316	                for stack_line in line.rstrip().splitlines():
317	                    headless_logger.essential(f"  {stack_line}")
318	
319	    def _dump_child_processes():
320	        """Use psutil (if available) to enumerate actual child processes."""
321	        try:
322	            import psutil
323	            current = psutil.Process()
324	            children = current.children(recursive=True)
325	            if not children:
326	                headless_logger.essential("[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] No child processes (psutil)")
327	                return
328	            for child in children:
329	                try:
330	                    headless_logger.essential(
331	                        f"[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] child pid={child.pid} name={child.name()} "
332	                        f"status={child.status()} cmdline={' '.join(child.cmdline()[:5])}"
333	                    )
334	                except (psutil.NoSuchProcess, psutil.AccessDenied):
335	                    headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] child pid={child.pid} (gone/access denied)")
336	        except ImportError:
337	            headless_logger.essential("[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] psutil not available — cannot enumerate children")
338	        except Exception as e:
339	            headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] child process enumeration failed: {e}")
340	
341	    def _full_shutdown_diagnostics(source_label: str):
342	        """Dump comprehensive diagnostics at shutdown time."""
343	        headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] === Diagnostics triggered by: {source_label} ===")
344	        headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] PID={os.getpid()} PPID={os.getppid()}")
345	        headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] Active threads: {_threading_mod.active_count()}")
346	        try:
347	            _dump_all_thread_stacks()
348	        except Exception as e:
349	            headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] thread stack dump failed: {e}")
350	        try:
351	            _dump_subprocess_registry()
352	        except Exception as e:
353	            headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] subprocess registry dump failed: {e}")
354	        try:
355	            _dump_child_processes()
356	        except Exception as e:
357	            headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN-DIAG] child process dump failed: {e}")
358	
359	    def _request_shutdown(signum, frame):
360	        # Log who raised us and the stack at the moment of the signal so that
361	        # unexpected shutdowns (e.g. someone else sending SIGINT/SIGTERM to this
362	        # console group) leave a forensic trail in the worker log.
363	        import traceback as _tb
364	        try:
365	            sig_name = signal.Signals(signum).name
366	        except (ValueError, AttributeError):
367	            sig_name = f"signum={signum}"
368	        headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN] {sig_name} received; stack at signal delivery:")
369	        for line in _tb.format_stack(frame):
370	            for stack_line in line.rstrip().splitlines():
371	                headless_logger.essential(f"  {stack_line}")
372	        _full_shutdown_diagnostics(f"signal {sig_name}")
373	        raise KeyboardInterrupt
374	
375	    # Steady-state SIGTERM/SIGINT map to the existing KeyboardInterrupt cleanup path.
376	    # SIGTERM during DB init, WGP import, or task_queue.start() still exits before
377	    # the later cleanup finally runs; that startup-window limitation is unchanged.
378	    signal.signal(signal.SIGTERM, _request_shutdown)
379	    signal.signal(signal.SIGINT, _request_shutdown)
380	
381	    # On Windows, console control events (Ctrl+C, Ctrl+Break, window close, logoff,
382	    # shutdown) are delivered to the console group rather than as POSIX signals.
383	    # Install a console-control handler so we can log the specific event that
384	    # caused the shutdown — next time "it just crashed" happens we will know
385	    # whether it was Ctrl+C, the window closing, the user logging off, etc.
386	    #
387	    # IMPORTANT: On Windows, CTRL_C_EVENT (code 0) can be triggered NOT only by
388	    # a user pressing Ctrl+C, but also by:
389	    #   1. GenerateConsoleCtrlEvent() called by any process in the console group
390	    #   2. A subprocess that crashes while sharing our console group
391	    #   3. Fortran/MKL runtimes (via numpy/scipy) installing their own handlers
392	    # The enhanced handler below logs thread stacks + child processes so we can
393	    # identify the true source.
394	    if os.name == "nt":
395	        try:
396	            import ctypes
397	            from ctypes import wintypes
398	            _CTRL_EVENT_NAMES = {
399	                0: "CTRL_C_EVENT",
400	                1: "CTRL_BREAK_EVENT",
401	                2: "CTRL_CLOSE_EVENT",
402	                5: "CTRL_LOGOFF_EVENT",
403	                6: "CTRL_SHUTDOWN_EVENT",
404	            }
405	            _HANDLER_ROUTINE = ctypes.WINFUNCTYPE(wintypes.BOOL, wintypes.DWORD)
406	
407	            def _win_ctrl_handler(event):
408	                name = _CTRL_EVENT_NAMES.get(event, f"UNKNOWN_EVENT_{event}")
409	                headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN] Windows console control event: {name} (code={event})")
410	                # Dump full diagnostics — this is the key forensic data.
411	                # The console handler runs on a dedicated OS thread, so we can
412	                # see what every Python thread was doing at the moment of the event.
413	                try:
414	                    _full_shutdown_diagnostics(f"console ctrl event {name}")
415	                except Exception as diag_err:
416	                    headless_logger.essential(f"[SHUTDOWN] diagnostics failed in ctrl handler: {diag_err}")
417	                # Return False so the default handler runs (which raises KI for
418	                # CTRL_C_EVENT and terminates the process for CTRL_CLOSE_EVENT etc.)
419	                return False
420	
421	            _ctrl_handler_ref = _HANDLER_ROUTINE(_win_ctrl_handler)
422	            kernel32 = ctypes.WinDLL("kernel32", use_last_error=True)
423	            if not kernel32.SetConsoleCtrlHandler(_ctrl_handler_ref, True):
424	                headless_logger.essential(f"SetConsoleCtrlHandler failed: {ctypes.get_last_error()}")
425	            # Keep a module-level reference to prevent GC collecting the callback
426	            globals()["_win_ctrl_handler_keepalive"] = _ctrl_handler_ref
427	
428	            # Log the console process group ID — helpful for understanding which
429	            # processes share our console and could send us CTRL_C_EVENT.
430	            try:
431	                _GetConsoleProcessList = kernel32.GetConsoleProcessList
432	                _GetConsoleProcessList.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(wintypes.DWORD), wintypes.DWORD]
433	                _GetConsoleProcessList.restype = wintypes.DWORD
434	                _pids_buf = (wintypes.DWORD * 64)()
435	                _n = _GetConsoleProcessList(_pids_buf, 64)
436	                _console_pids = [_pids_buf[i] for i in range(_n)]
437	                headless_logger.essential(
438	                    f"[STARTUP] Console group PIDs (count={_n}): {_console_pids}; our PID={os.getpid()}"
439	                )
440	            except Exception as e:
441	                headless_logger.essential(f"[STARTUP] Could not enumerate console group: {e}")
442	
443	        except (OSError, AttributeError, ImportError) as e:
444	            headless_logger.essential(f"Could not install console control handler: {e}")
445	
446	    headless_logger.essential("bootstrap done")

700	                    if isinstance(raw_result, TaskResult):

705	                        result = None
706	
707	                    if task_succeeded:

738	                            cleanup_generated_files(output_location, current_task_id, debug_mode)
739	                            anchor.set(output=output_location)
740	                    else:
741	                        error_message = (result.error_message if result else output_location) or "Unknown error"
742	                        anchor.set(error=error_message)
743	                        is_retryable, error_category, max_attempts = is_retryable_error(error_message)

746	                        if is_retryable and current_attempts < max_attempts:
747	                            headless_logger.warning(

750	                            )
751	                            requeue_task_for_retry(current_task_id, error_message, current_attempts, error_category)
752	                        else:

760	            except Exception as e:

769	                    current_attempts = task_info.get("attempts", 0)
770	
771	                    if is_retryable and current_attempts < max_attempts:
772	                        headless_logger.warning(

775	                        )
776	                        requeue_task_for_retry(current_task_id, error_message, current_attempts, error_category)

793	                _display.on_task_done()

829	            except BaseException as _cleanup_err:

834	            except BaseException as _cleanup_err:
835	                headless_logger.debug_anomaly("SHUTDOWN", f"task_queue.stop failed: {_cleanup_err}")
```

```
26	def _stream_download_to_path(url: str, destination_path: Path, timeout: int = 300) -> None:
27	    with requests.get(url, stream=True, timeout=timeout) as response:
28	        response.raise_for_status()
29	        destination_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
30	        with open(destination_path, "wb") as handle:
31	            for chunk in response.iter_content(chunk_size=8192):
32	                if chunk:
33	                    handle.write(chunk)
34	
35	def _get_unique_target_path(target_dir: Path, base_name: str, extension: str) -> Path:
36	    """Generates a unique target Path in the given directory by appending a timestamp and random string."""
37	    target_dir = Path(target_dir)
38	    target_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
39	    timestamp_short = datetime.now().strftime("%H%M%S")
40	    # Use a short UUID/random string to significantly reduce collision probability with just timestamp
41	    unique_suffix = uuid.uuid4().hex[:6]
42	
43	    # Construct the filename
44	    # Ensure extension has a leading dot
45	    if extension and not extension.startswith('.'):
46	        extension = '.' + extension
47	
48	    filename = f"{base_name}_{timestamp_short}_{unique_suffix}{extension}"
49	    return target_dir / filename
50	
51	def download_file(url, dest_folder, filename):
52	    from source.utils.lora_validation import validate_lora_file
53	
54	    dest_path = Path(dest_folder) / filename
55	    if dest_path.exists():
56	        # Validate existing file before assuming it's good
57	        if filename.endswith('.safetensors') or 'lora' in filename.lower():
58	            is_valid, validation_msg = validate_lora_file(dest_path, filename)
59	            if is_valid:
60	                headless_logger.essential(f"File {filename} already exists and is valid in {dest_folder}. {validation_msg}")
61	                return True
62	            else:
63	                headless_logger.warning(f"Existing file {filename} failed validation ({validation_msg}). Re-downloading...")
64	                dest_path.unlink()
65	        else:
66	            headless_logger.essential(f"File {filename} already exists in {dest_folder}.")
67	            return True
68	
69	    # Use huggingface_hub for HuggingFace URLs for better reliability
70	    if "huggingface.co" in url:
71	        try:
72	            from huggingface_hub import hf_hub_download
73	
74	            # Parse HuggingFace URL to extract repo_id and filename
75	            # Format: https://huggingface.co/USER/REPO/resolve/BRANCH/FILENAME
76	            parsed = urlparse(url)
77	            path_parts = parsed.path.strip('/').split('/')
78	
79	            if len(path_parts) >= 4 and path_parts[2] == 'resolve':
80	                repo_id = f"{path_parts[0]}/{path_parts[1]}"
81	                branch = path_parts[3] if len(path_parts) > 4 else "main"
82	                hf_filename = '/'.join(path_parts[4:]) if len(path_parts) > 4 else filename
83	
84	                headless_logger.essential(f"Downloading {filename} from HuggingFace repo {repo_id} using hf_hub_download...")
85	
86	                # Download using huggingface_hub with automatic checksums and resumption
87	                downloaded_path = hf_hub_download(
88	                    repo_id=repo_id,
89	                    filename=hf_filename,
90	                    revision=branch,
91	                    cache_dir=str(dest_folder),
92	                    resume_download=True,
93	                    local_files_only=False
94	                )
95	
96	                # Copy from HF cache to target location if different
97	                if Path(downloaded_path) != dest_path:
98	                    dest_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
99	                    shutil.copy2(downloaded_path, dest_path)
100	
101	                # Validate the downloaded file
102	                if filename.endswith('.safetensors') or 'lora' in filename.lower():
103	                    is_valid, validation_msg = validate_lora_file(dest_path, filename)
104	                    if not is_valid:
105	                        headless_logger.error(f"Downloaded file {filename} failed validation: {validation_msg}")
106	                        dest_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
107	                        return False
108	                    headless_logger.essential(f"Successfully downloaded and validated {filename}. {validation_msg}")
109	                else:
110	                    headless_logger.essential(f"Successfully downloaded {filename} with integrity verification.")
111	                return True
112	
113	        except ImportError:
114	            headless_logger.warning(f"huggingface_hub not available, falling back to requests for {url}")
115	        except (OSError, ValueError, requests.RequestException) as e:
116	            headless_logger.warning(f"HuggingFace download failed for {filename}: {e}, falling back to requests")
117	
118	    # Fallback to requests with basic integrity checks
119	    try:
120	        headless_logger.essential(f"Downloading {filename} from {url} to {dest_folder}...")
121	        response = requests.get(url, stream=True)
122	        response.raise_for_status() # Raise an exception for HTTP errors
123	
124	        # Get expected content length for verification
125	        expected_size = int(response.headers.get('content-length', 0))
126	
127	        dest_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
128	        with open(dest_path, 'wb') as f:
129	            downloaded_size = 0
130	            for chunk in response.iter_content(chunk_size=8192):
131	                f.write(chunk)
132	                downloaded_size += len(chunk)
133	
134	        # Verify download integrity
135	        actual_size = dest_path.stat().st_size
136	        if expected_size > 0 and actual_size != expected_size:
137	            headless_logger.error(f"Size mismatch for {filename}: expected {expected_size}, got {actual_size}")
138	            dest_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
139	            return False
140	
141	        # Use comprehensive validation for LoRA files
142	        if filename.endswith('.safetensors') or 'lora' in filename.lower():
143	            is_valid, validation_msg = validate_lora_file(dest_path, filename)
144	            if not is_valid:
145	                headless_logger.error(f"Downloaded file {filename} failed validation: {validation_msg}")
146	                dest_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
147	                return False
148	            headless_logger.essential(f"Successfully downloaded and validated {filename}. {validation_msg}")
149	        else:
150	            # For non-LoRA safetensors files, do basic format check
151	            if filename.endswith('.safetensors'):
152	                try:
153	                    import safetensors.torch as st
154	                    with st.safe_open(dest_path, framework="pt") as f:
155	                        pass  # Just verify it can be opened
156	                    headless_logger.essential(f"Successfully downloaded and verified safetensors file {filename}.")
157	                except ImportError:
158	                    headless_logger.debug_anomaly("WARNING", f"safetensors not available for verification of {filename}")
159	                except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
160	                    headless_logger.error(f"Downloaded safetensors file {filename} appears corrupted: {e}")
161	                    dest_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
162	                    return False
163	            else:
164	                headless_logger.essential(f"Successfully downloaded {filename}.")
165	
166	        return True
167	
168	    except (OSError, requests.RequestException, ValueError) as e:
169	        headless_logger.error(f"Failed to download {filename}: {e}")
170	        if dest_path.exists(): # Attempt to clean up partial download
171	            try: os.remove(dest_path)
172	            except OSError as e_cleanup:
173	                headless_logger.warning(f"Failed to clean up partial download {dest_path}: {e_cleanup}")
174	        return False
175	
176	def _download_file_if_url(
177	    file_url_or_path: str,
178	    download_target_dir: Path | str | None,
179	    task_id_for_logging: str | None = "generic_task",
180	    descriptive_name: str | None = None,
181	    default_extension: str = ".jpg",
182	    default_stem: str = "file",
183	    file_type_label: str = "file",
184	    timeout: int = 300
185	) -> str:
186	    """
187	    Generic function to download a file from URL if needed.
188	
189	    Args:
190	        file_url_or_path: URL or local path
191	        download_target_dir: Directory to save downloaded file
192	        task_id_for_logging: Task ID for logging
193	        descriptive_name: Optional descriptive name for the file
194	        default_extension: Default file extension if none detected
195	        default_stem: Default stem for filename if none detected
196	        file_type_label: Label for logging (e.g., "image", "video")
197	        timeout: Request timeout in seconds
198	
199	    Returns:
200	        Local file path string if downloaded, otherwise returns the original string
201	    """
202	    if not file_url_or_path:
203	        return file_url_or_path
204	
205	    parsed_url = urlparse(file_url_or_path)
206	    if parsed_url.scheme in ['http', 'https'] and download_target_dir:
207	        target_dir_path = Path(download_target_dir)
208	        try:
209	            target_dir_path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
210	            headless_logger.debug(f"Task {task_id_for_logging}: Downloading {file_type_label} from URL: {file_url_or_path} to {target_dir_path.resolve()}")
211	
212	            original_filename = Path(parsed_url.path).name
213	            original_suffix = Path(original_filename).suffix if Path(original_filename).suffix else default_extension
214	            if not original_suffix.startswith('.'):
215	                original_suffix = '.' + original_suffix
216	
217	            # Use descriptive naming if provided, otherwise fall back to improved default
218	            if descriptive_name:
219	                base_name_for_download = descriptive_name[:50]  # Limit length
220	            else:
221	                # Improved default naming
222	                cleaned_stem = Path(original_filename).stem[:30] if Path(original_filename).stem else default_stem
223	                base_name_for_download = f"input_{cleaned_stem}"
224	
225	            # _get_unique_target_path expects a Path object for target_dir
226	            local_file_path = _get_unique_target_path(target_dir_path, base_name_for_download, original_suffix)
227	            _stream_download_to_path(file_url_or_path, local_file_path, timeout=timeout)
228	
229	            headless_logger.debug(f"Task {task_id_for_logging}: Successfully downloaded {file_type_label} to {local_file_path}")
230	            return str(local_file_path)
231	
232	        except (requests.exceptions.RequestException, Exception) as e:
233	            headless_logger.debug(f"Task {task_id_for_logging}: ERROR downloading {file_type_label} from {file_url_or_path}: {e}")
234	            raise DownloadError(str(e)) from e
235	        except OSError as e_dl:
236	            headless_logger.debug(f"Task {task_id_for_logging}: ERROR saving {file_type_label} to {target_dir_path}: {e_dl}", exc_info=True)
237	            raise DownloadError(str(e_dl)) from e_dl
238	    else:
239	        # Not a URL, or no download directory provided
240	        return file_url_or_path
241	
242	def download_video_if_url(video_url_or_path: str, download_target_dir: Path | str | None, task_id_for_logging: str | None = "generic_task", descriptive_name: str | None = None) -> str:
```

```
1	"""Download and extract motion frames from pre-warped structure videos."""
2	
3	import sys
4	import numpy as np
5	from pathlib import Path
6	from typing import List
7	from source.core.constants import BYTES_PER_MB
8	from source.core.log import generation_logger
9	
10	__all__ = [
11	    "download_and_extract_motion_frames",
12	]
13	
14	
15	def download_and_extract_motion_frames(
16	    structure_motion_video_url: str,
17	    frame_start: int,
18	    frame_count: int,
19	    download_dir: Path,
20	) -> List[np.ndarray]:
21	    """
22	    Download pre-warped structure motion video and extract needed frames.
23	
24	    This is the segment-level function that downloads the orchestrator's
25	    pre-computed motion video and extracts only the frames this segment needs.
26	
27	    Args:
28	        structure_motion_video_url: URL or path to the pre-warped motion video
29	        frame_start: Starting frame index to extract
30	        frame_count: Number of frames to extract
31	        download_dir: Directory to download video to
32	
33	    Returns:
34	        List of numpy arrays [H, W, C] uint8 RGB
35	
36	    Raises:
37	        ValueError: If video cannot be downloaded or frames extracted
38	    """
39	    from urllib.parse import urlparse
40	    import requests
41	
42	    try:
43	        # Determine if this is a URL or local path
44	        parsed = urlparse(structure_motion_video_url)
45	        is_url = parsed.scheme in ['http', 'https']
46	
47	        if is_url:
48	            # Download the video
49	            download_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
50	            local_video_path = download_dir / "structure_motion.mp4"
51	
52	            response = requests.get(structure_motion_video_url, timeout=120)
53	            response.raise_for_status()
54	
55	            with open(local_video_path, 'wb') as f:
56	                f.write(response.content)
57	        else:
58	            # Use local path (support plain paths and file:// URLs)
59	            if parsed.scheme == "file":
60	                local_video_path = Path(parsed.path)
61	            else:
62	                local_video_path = Path(structure_motion_video_url)
63	            if not local_video_path.exists():
64	                raise ValueError(f"Structure motion video not found: {local_video_path}")
65	
66	        # Extract frames (prefer decord, fall back to cv2)
67	
68	        try:
69	            # Add Wan2GP to path (decord often installed alongside this stack)
70	            wan_dir = Path(__file__).parent.parent.parent.parent / "Wan2GP"
71	            if str(wan_dir) not in sys.path:
72	                sys.path.insert(0, str(wan_dir))
73	
74	            import decord  # type: ignore
75	            decord.bridge.set_bridge('torch')
76	
77	            vr = decord.VideoReader(str(local_video_path))
78	            total_frames = len(vr)
79	
80	            if frame_start >= total_frames:
81	                raise ValueError(f"frame_start {frame_start} >= total frames {total_frames}")
82	
83	            actual_frame_count = min(frame_count, total_frames - frame_start)
84	            if actual_frame_count < frame_count:
85	                generation_logger.warning(f"[STRUCTURE_MOTION] Only {actual_frame_count} frames available (requested {frame_count})")
86	
87	            frame_indices = list(range(frame_start, frame_start + actual_frame_count))
88	            frames_tensor = vr.get_batch(frame_indices)  # torch tensor [T, H, W, C]
89	
90	            frames_list: List[np.ndarray] = []
91	            for i in range(len(frames_tensor)):
92	                frame_np = frames_tensor[i].cpu().numpy()
93	                if frame_np.dtype != np.uint8:
94	                    frame_np = np.clip(frame_np, 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
95	                frames_list.append(frame_np)
96	
97	            generation_logger.debug_block(
98	                "STRUCTURE_MOTION_EXTRACT",
99	                {
100	                    "frames": len(frames_list),
101	                    "backend": "decord",
102	                    "source": str(local_video_path),
103	                },
104	            )
105	            return frames_list
106	
107	        except ModuleNotFoundError:
108	            import cv2
109	
110	            cap = cv2.VideoCapture(str(local_video_path))
111	            if not cap.isOpened():
112	                raise ValueError(f"cv2 failed to open video: {local_video_path}")
113	
114	            total_frames = int(cap.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_COUNT)) or 0
115	            if total_frames <= 0:
116	                # Some codecs don't report frame count; we'll read until EOF.
117	                total_frames = 10**9
118	
119	            if frame_start >= total_frames:
120	                cap.release()
121	                raise ValueError(f"frame_start {frame_start} >= total frames {total_frames}")
122	
123	            # Seek to frame_start
124	            cap.set(cv2.CAP_PROP_POS_FRAMES, frame_start)
125	
126	            frames_list: List[np.ndarray] = []
127	            for _ in range(frame_count):
128	                ok, frame_bgr = cap.read()
129	                if not ok:
130	                    break
131	                frame_rgb = cv2.cvtColor(frame_bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
132	                if frame_rgb.dtype != np.uint8:
133	                    frame_rgb = np.clip(frame_rgb, 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
134	                frames_list.append(frame_rgb)
135	
136	            cap.release()
137	            generation_logger.debug_block(
138	                "STRUCTURE_MOTION_EXTRACT",
139	                {
140	                    "frames": len(frames_list),
141	                    "backend": "cv2",
142	                    "source": str(local_video_path),
143	                },
144	            )
145	            return frames_list
146	
147	    except (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as e:
148	        generation_logger.error(f"[ERROR] Failed to extract motion frames: {e}", exc_info=True)
149	        raise
150
```

```json
{
  "plan": "# Implementation Plan: Local-worker HTTP endpoints + `file://` input handling\n\n## Overview\n\nGoal: enable the dormant local-worker fast-path. The Python worker currently has no HTTP surface and treats `file://` URLs as opaque local paths. We will add a tiny stdlib-based HTTP server (three endpoints: `/health`, `/ingest`, `/cleanup`) that runs in a background thread alongside the polling loop in PAT mode only, plus teach `source/utils/download_utils.py` to recognize `file://` URLs whose paths live inside the materialization dir.\n\nRepo shape relevant here:\n- Polling worker entrypoint: `source/runtime/worker/server.py:main()` (`server.py:248-836`). The cloud-vs-PAT discriminator is the auth-mode resolver at `server.py:47-64` — we reuse its env signal (`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`) to gate startup.\n- URL fetcher: `source/utils/download_utils.py:_download_file_if_url` at `download_utils.py:202-240` — `parsed_url.scheme in ['http','https']` is the only URL gate; `file://` falls through to \"return original string\" and breaks downstream `Path` users.\n- Reference for `file://` handling: `source/media/structure/download.py:57-64` — strips scheme via `urlparse(...).path`. We follow the same shape but add a strict allowed-dir check, since this code path receives untrusted task params.\n- Test framework: `pytest` (see `pyproject.toml:150-158`); existing tests live under `tests/`. `fastapi`+`python-multipart` are already deps (`pyproject.toml:84-85`), but stdlib `http.server` is sufficient and matches the brief's preference, so no new deps.\n\nConstraints baked in: bind `127.0.0.1` only; require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` on `/ingest` + `/cleanup` (not `/health`); refuse path-traversal; do not start the server when `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` is present; persist `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (0600) and `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` for the reigh-app side to read.\n\n## Main Phase\n\n### Step 1: Add the local HTTP server module (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n\n1. **Create** `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py` exposing two callables: `start_local_http_server(*, materialization_dir: Path, port: int | None, worker_id: str, version: str) -> LocalHttpServer` and `LocalHttpServer.shutdown()`.\n2. **Implement** with `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer` bound to `(\"127.0.0.1\", port_or_0)` plus a `BaseHTTPRequestHandler` subclass:\n   - `GET /health` → `200 application/json` `{\"ok\": true, \"worker_id\": ..., \"version\": ...}`. No auth check.\n   - `POST /ingest` → require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (constant-time compare via `hmac.compare_digest`). Parse `multipart/form-data` with `cgi.FieldStorage` (stdlib) reading exactly one file part named `file`. Pick filename suffix from the upload's filename (sanitize: keep only `[A-Za-z0-9.]`, last `.ext` only). Write to `materialization_dir / f\"{uuid4().hex}{suffix}\"`. Respond `200` with `{\"path\": \"<abs>\", \"size\": <bytes>}`.\n   - `POST /cleanup` → require Bearer token. Read JSON `{\"path\": \"...\"}`. Resolve via `Path(path).resolve()` and require it is `relative_to(materialization_dir.resolve())` (catches `..`, symlink escapes — use `resolve(strict=False)` and `is_relative_to`). If resolved file is outside the dir, respond `400`. If it doesn't exist, respond `204` (idempotent). Else `unlink()` + `204`.\n   - Any other path/method → `404`.\n   - Override `log_message` to route through `headless_logger.debug` (avoid noisy stderr).\n3. **Generate** the auth token with `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` at server construction.\n4. **Persist** state: ensure `~/.reigh-local-worker/` exists (mode 0700); write `auth-token` with `os.open(... O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC, 0o600)`; write `port` (the actually-bound port from `server.server_address[1]`) as plain text. On `shutdown()`, best-effort delete both.\n5. **Ensure** materialization dir exists with mode 0700 (created via `Path.mkdir(mode=0o700, exist_ok=True)`; on POSIX additionally `os.chmod(0o700)` if it pre-existed).\n6. **Run** `serve_forever()` on a daemon thread; `shutdown()` calls `server.shutdown()` then `server.server_close()` and joins the thread with a short timeout.\n\n### Step 2: Wire the server into the worker lifecycle (`source/runtime/worker/server.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Add** a tiny helper near `_resolve_worker_db_client_key` (around `server.py:47`):\n   ```python\n   def _is_local_worker_mode() -> bool:\n       return not (os.environ.get(\"SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY\") or os.environ.get(\"SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY\"))\n   ```\n   This is the single source of truth — reuses the same env signals the existing resolver consults at `server.py:49`.\n2. **Start** the server in `main()` after `_initialize_db_runtime` succeeds (`server.py:500`) and before `task_queue.start()`. Guard with `_is_local_worker_mode()`. Resolve port from `int(os.environ.get(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT\", \"0\"))` — `0` means \"let the OS pick\", but we'll default the env to a stable port (see assumption). Resolve materialization dir from `os.environ.get(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR\", str(Path.home() / \".reigh-local-files\"))`.\n3. **Bind** the returned `LocalHttpServer` to a local variable in `main()` and ensure shutdown is called in the existing `finally` block (`server.py:804-835`), guarded by `if local_http_server:` and wrapped in `try/except BaseException` consistent with the surrounding cleanup style.\n4. **Log** startup once via `headless_logger.essential(f\"Local HTTP server listening on 127.0.0.1:{port}, materializing to {dir}\")`.\n\n### Step 3: Teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` (`source/utils/download_utils.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Extend** the URL gate at `download_utils.py:205-206` so the function recognizes `file://` separately. After `parsed_url = urlparse(...)`:\n   - If `parsed_url.scheme == \"file\"`: resolve the path with `Path(parsed_url.path).resolve(strict=False)`, require it lives under `_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir().resolve()` (use `is_relative_to`), require `exists()` and `is_file()`. On success, return the resolved path string without copying. On failure, raise `DownloadError(f\"file:// path not allowed: {file_url_or_path}\")` — the worker's existing error path already converts that to a task failure.\n   - Existing `http`/`https` branch is unchanged.\n2. **Add** module-level `_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir() -> Path` returning `Path(os.environ.get(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR\", Path.home() / \".reigh-local-files\")).expanduser()`. Read each call (cheap; supports tests changing the env). Do NOT cache.\n3. **Apply** automatically: because `download_video_if_url` and `download_image_if_url` both delegate to `_download_file_if_url`, the single change covers both call sites — confirmed by reading `download_utils.py:248-277`.\n4. **Leave** the `download_target_dir is None` \"not a URL, return as-is\" branch alone; `file://` URLs are now handled before that branch.\n\n### Step 4: Tests for the HTTP server (`tests/test_local_http.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n\n1. **Create** `tests/test_local_http.py`. Each test starts a `LocalHttpServer` on an ephemeral port (`port=0`), with `materialization_dir=tmp_path / \"mat\"`, then `urllib.request` against `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`. Use `secrets.token_urlsafe`-derived token from the server.\n2. **Cover** (each as its own `test_*` function):\n   - `/health` returns 200 with the expected JSON, no `Authorization` header sent.\n   - `/ingest` without `Authorization` → 401.\n   - `/ingest` with wrong bearer → 401.\n   - `/ingest` with valid bearer + `multipart/form-data` (use `email.mime` or hand-rolled multipart since stdlib has no client; alternatively use `requests` since it's already a dep) → 200, returned `path` exists, `size` matches uploaded bytes, file lives under `materialization_dir`.\n   - `/cleanup` with `{\"path\": \"/etc/passwd\"}` → 400, `/etc/passwd` untouched (just check response code; never actually attempt deletion since the handler rejects pre-`unlink`).\n   - `/cleanup` with `{\"path\": \"<materialization_dir>/../escape\"}` → 400.\n   - `/cleanup` deletes a real materialized file → 204; second call on the same path → 204 (idempotent).\n3. **Use** `pytest` fixtures to start/stop the server cleanly per test; assert background thread exits on `shutdown()`.\n\n### Step 5: Tests for `file://` handling and lifecycle gating (`tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py`, `tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Create** `tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py`:\n   - `test_download_image_accepts_file_url_inside_materialization_dir`: write a fake file inside `tmp_path`, set `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR=tmp_path` via `monkeypatch`, call `download_image_if_url(f\"file://{path}\", download_target_dir=tmp_path/\"out\")`, assert returned string equals the resolved local path and the file was NOT copied.\n   - Same shape for `download_video_if_url`.\n   - `test_rejects_file_url_outside_materialization_dir`: `tmp_path/\"in\"` is the mat dir, file lives in `tmp_path/\"out\"`, expect `DownloadError`.\n   - `test_rejects_file_url_with_traversal`: pass `file:///etc/passwd` with mat dir set to a tmp dir → `DownloadError`.\n2. **Create** `tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py`:\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_true_with_only_pat`: `monkeypatch.delenv(\"SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY\", raising=False)` etc., assert `_is_local_worker_mode() is True`.\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_in_cloud`: set `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=...`, assert `False`.         Idea:
```
# Local-worker HTTP endpoints + file:// input handling

This task lives in the **reigh-worker** repo (a Python project that polls a Supabase queue for tasks and executes them on GPU). It's the worker side of the local-file-handle work that already shipped on the reigh-app side.

## Background (why this exists)

reigh-app now has two flows for getting a user's local file (a `FileSystemFileHandle` persisted in the browser) into a worker task as input:

- **Remote worker fallback (already shipped, used today)**: app uploads the bytes to Supabase Storage, puts a public URL into task params, worker downloads from the URL.
- **Local worker fast path (DORMANT — what this task enables)**: when the worker is running locally on the same machine as the user's browser, the resolver in `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/{healthcheck,ingest}.ts` is supposed to:
  1. probe `http://localhost:<port>/health` to detect the worker
  2. POST file bytes to `http://localhost:<port>/ingest`, receive back `{ path: "/abs/path/on/disk" }`
  3. put `file://<path>` into task params
  4. after task completion, POST to `http://localhost:<port>/cleanup` with the path so the worker deletes the materialized copy

The worker today has no HTTP server — it's a polling client (`source/runtime/worker/server.py` despite its name is not an HTTP server). It also can't read `file://` URLs in most code paths (see #2 below).

This task is what makes the local fast-path actually work end-to-end.

## What to build

### 1. HTTP server with three endpoints in reigh-worker

Add a small HTTP server that runs alongside the existing polling worker process. Endpoints:

- `GET /health` — returns 200 + JSON `{"ok": true, "worker_id": "...", "version": "..."}`. Cheap, no auth check beyond localhost.
- `POST /ingest` — accepts `multipart/form-data` with one file part. Writes the bytes to a known materialization dir (e.g. `~/.reigh-local-files/<uuid>.<ext>`), returns 200 + `{"path": "/abs/path", "size": <bytes>}`. Auth: see below.
- `POST /cleanup` — accepts JSON `{"path": "/abs/path"}`. Validates the path is inside the materialization dir (no escapes), deletes it, returns 204. Auth: see below.

**Auth model**: bind to `127.0.0.1` only (NOT `0.0.0.0`). Even on localhost, an attacker process on the same machine could hit the endpoints — so require a shared secret on `/ingest` and `/cleanup` via `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header. The token is generated at worker startup, written to a 0600 file at `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (owner-only readable). The reigh-app side reads that file before making requests.

`/health` does NOT require the token (otherwise the probe can't tell "worker present" from "wrong token"); it leaks zero sensitive info.

**Server framework**: pick whichever is least invasive — stdlib `http.server` is fine for this scale (<10 req/sec, single user). Avoid pulling in FastAPI/aiohttp unless they're already present in worker deps. Investigate `pyproject.toml` / `requirements.txt` first.

**Lifecycle**: server runs in a background thread spun up at worker startup, dies cleanly on shutdown (SIGINT/SIGTERM). It's PAT-mode-only — when the worker is launched with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (cloud mode), do NOT start the server. Detection logic exists in `source/runtime/worker/server.py:_resolve_worker_db_client_key()` — reuse it, don't duplicate.

**Port**: configurable via env var (e.g. `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT`), default to a sensible high port. Write the chosen port to `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` so reigh-app can find it (alongside the auth-token file).

**Materialization dir**: configurable env var (e.g. `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR`), default to `~/.reigh-local-files`. Create with mode 0700 if missing. Hard cap: never write outside this dir; never accept a path component containing `..`.

### 2. file:// support in download_utils.py

`source/utils/download_utils.py` is what the worker uses to fetch task input bytes. Currently it handles `http://` and `https://` URLs by streaming with `requests.get()`. For `file://` URLs (which the new local fast-path produces), it does NOT strip the scheme — it treats `file:///path/to/foo.jpg` as a literal local path, which fails in most call sites.

Fix: in the URL-detection helper (currently around line 205–206), recognize `file://` as a URL scheme. When the scheme is `file`, strip the scheme prefix to get the local path, validate that the path exists and is inside an allowed dir (see materialization dir above — only allow file:// inputs that point inside that dir), then return the path WITHOUT downloading. Don't copy the file; the worker is allowed to read it in place.

Apply this to BOTH `download_image_if_url()` and `download_video_if_url()` (and any siblings). There's apparently a working `file://` handler at `source/media/structure/download.py` — read it for reference but don't depend on it (the image/video paths are the high-value ones).

### 3. Wire the cleanup hook to actually fire

The reigh-app side's `complete_task` edge function calls back into the worker's `/cleanup` for each materialized input. Verify that wiring works end-to-end. If the edge function passes the materialized paths through some indirection, make sure the cleanup actually reaches the right worker.

The edge function code is in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts` (already shipped). Read it to understand the contract — what URL does it call, what payload does it send, what does it expect back?

## Tests

- `/health` returns 200, no auth.
- `/ingest` rejects without auth token (401).
- `/ingest` rejects with wrong token (401).
- `/ingest` accepts multipart upload, writes file, returns path.
- `/cleanup` rejects path-traversal (`/etc/passwd`, `../../foo`).
- `/cleanup` deletes valid file, returns 204.
- `/cleanup` is idempotent (already-deleted file → 204, not 500).
- `download_image_if_url` and `download_video_if_url` accept `file://` paths inside materialization dir.
- Both reject `file://` paths outside materialization dir (security — don't let task params point at arbitrary local files).
- Server doesn't start when `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is set (cloud mode).
- Server starts when only PAT is set (local mode).

Use the worker's existing test framework (likely pytest — check `pyproject.toml`).

## Constraints

- Don't refactor unrelated code. Don't add new abstractions. Don't add comments explaining the change.
- Reuse existing helpers — particularly the auth-mode detection in `source/runtime/worker/server.py`.
- Don't pull in heavyweight HTTP frameworks. `http.server` from stdlib is sufficient. If you genuinely need something more, justify it in the plan.
- Keep the server code in a clearly-named module (e.g. `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`).

## Out of scope

- The reigh-app side is already done — don't touch it unless you find a real bug in `localWorker/{healthcheck,ingest}.ts` that blocks the integration. Flag, don't fix.
- Don't add metrics, logging frameworks, prometheus, etc. Plain `print()` or whatever the worker already uses is fine.

## Verification

- Run the test suite (`pytest` or whatever the project uses).
- Manually trace the auth flow end-to-end: server starts → token + port written → reigh-app can read both → `/health` reachable → `/ingest` accepts an authed upload → `/cleanup` removes it.
- Confirm cloud-mode launch (with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`) does not start the server.

## Deliverables

- New module(s) for the HTTP server, with tests.
- `download_utils.py` updated for `file://` with tests.
- Lifecycle integration into worker startup/shutdown.
- A short note in your final summary describing: which port + dir defaults you chose, where the auth token + port file live on disk, any places the reigh-app side will need a small follow-up tweak.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Local-worker HTTP endpoints + `file://` input handling

## Overview

Goal: enable the dormant local-worker fast-path. The Python worker currently has no HTTP surface and treats `file://` URLs as opaque local paths. We will add a tiny stdlib-based HTTP server (three endpoints: `/health`, `/ingest`, `/cleanup`) that runs in a background thread alongside the polling loop in PAT mode only, plus teach `source/utils/download_utils.py` to recognize `file://` URLs whose paths live inside the materialization dir.

Repo shape relevant here:
- Polling worker entrypoint: `source/runtime/worker/server.py:main()` (`server.py:248-836`). The cloud-vs-PAT discriminator is the auth-mode resolver at `server.py:47-64` — we reuse its env signal (`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`) to gate startup.
- URL fetcher: `source/utils/download_utils.py:_download_file_if_url` at `download_utils.py:202-240` — `parsed_url.scheme in ['http','https']` is the only URL gate; `file://` falls through to "return original string" and breaks downstream `Path` users.
- Reference for `file://` handling: `source/media/structure/download.py:57-64` — strips scheme via `urlparse(...).path`. We follow the same shape but add a strict allowed-dir check, since this code path receives untrusted task params.
- Test framework: `pytest` (see `pyproject.toml:150-158`); existing tests live under `tests/`. `fastapi`+`python-multipart` are already deps (`pyproject.toml:84-85`), but stdlib `http.server` is sufficient and matches the brief's preference, so no new deps.

Constraints baked in: bind `127.0.0.1` only; require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` on `/ingest` + `/cleanup` (not `/health`); refuse path-traversal; do not start the server when `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` is present; persist `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (0600) and `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` for the reigh-app side to read.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Add the local HTTP server module (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py` exposing two callables: `start_local_http_server(*, materialization_dir: Path, port: int | None, worker_id: str, version: str) -> LocalHttpServer` and `LocalHttpServer.shutdown()`.
2. **Implement** with `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer` bound to `("127.0.0.1", port_or_0)` plus a `BaseHTTPRequestHandler` subclass:
   - `GET /health` → `200 application/json` `{"ok": true, "worker_id": ..., "version": ...}`. No auth check.
   - `POST /ingest` → require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (constant-time compare via `hmac.compare_digest`). Parse `multipart/form-data` with `cgi.FieldStorage` (stdlib) reading exactly one file part named `file`. Pick filename suffix from the upload's filename (sanitize: keep only `[A-Za-z0-9.]`, last `.ext` only). Write to `materialization_dir / f"{uuid4().hex}{suffix}"`. Respond `200` with `{"path": "<abs>", "size": <bytes>}`.
   - `POST /cleanup` → require Bearer token. Read JSON `{"path": "..."}`. Resolve via `Path(path).resolve()` and require it is `relative_to(materialization_dir.resolve())` (catches `..`, symlink escapes — use `resolve(strict=False)` and `is_relative_to`). If resolved file is outside the dir, respond `400`. If it doesn't exist, respond `204` (idempotent). Else `unlink()` + `204`.
   - Any other path/method → `404`.
   - Override `log_message` to route through `headless_logger.debug` (avoid noisy stderr).
3. **Generate** the auth token with `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` at server construction.
4. **Persist** state: ensure `~/.reigh-local-worker/` exists (mode 0700); write `auth-token` with `os.open(... O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC, 0o600)`; write `port` (the actually-bound port from `server.server_address[1]`) as plain text. On `shutdown()`, best-effort delete both.
5. **Ensure** materialization dir exists with mode 0700 (created via `Path.mkdir(mode=0o700, exist_ok=True)`; on POSIX additionally `os.chmod(0o700)` if it pre-existed).
6. **Run** `serve_forever()` on a daemon thread; `shutdown()` calls `server.shutdown()` then `server.server_close()` and joins the thread with a short timeout.

### Step 2: Wire the server into the worker lifecycle (`source/runtime/worker/server.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** a tiny helper near `_resolve_worker_db_client_key` (around `server.py:47`):
   ```python
   def _is_local_worker_mode() -> bool:
       return not (os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") or os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY"))
   ```
   This is the single source of truth — reuses the same env signals the existing resolver consults at `server.py:49`.
2. **Start** the server in `main()` after `_initialize_db_runtime` succeeds (`server.py:500`) and before `task_queue.start()`. Guard with `_is_local_worker_mode()`. Resolve port from `int(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT", "0"))` — `0` means "let the OS pick", but we'll default the env to a stable port (see assumption). Resolve materialization dir from `os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR", str(Path.home() / ".reigh-local-files"))`.
3. **Bind** the returned `LocalHttpServer` to a local variable in `main()` and ensure shutdown is called in the existing `finally` block (`server.py:804-835`), guarded by `if local_http_server:` and wrapped in `try/except BaseException` consistent with the surrounding cleanup style.
4. **Log** startup once via `headless_logger.essential(f"Local HTTP server listening on 127.0.0.1:{port}, materializing to {dir}")`.

### Step 3: Teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` (`source/utils/download_utils.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Extend** the URL gate at `download_utils.py:205-206` so the function recognizes `file://` separately. After `parsed_url = urlparse(...)`:
   - If `parsed_url.scheme == "file"`: resolve the path with `Path(parsed_url.path).resolve(strict=False)`, require it lives under `_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir().resolve()` (use `is_relative_to`), require `exists()` and `is_file()`. On success, return the resolved path string without copying. On failure, raise `DownloadError(f"file:// path not allowed: {file_url_or_path}")` — the worker's existing error path already converts that to a task failure.
   - Existing `http`/`https` branch is unchanged.
2. **Add** module-level `_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir() -> Path` returning `Path(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR", Path.home() / ".reigh-local-files")).expanduser()`. Read each call (cheap; supports tests changing the env). Do NOT cache.
3. **Apply** automatically: because `download_video_if_url` and `download_image_if_url` both delegate to `_download_file_if_url`, the single change covers both call sites — confirmed by reading `download_utils.py:248-277`.
4. **Leave** the `download_target_dir is None` "not a URL, return as-is" branch alone; `file://` URLs are now handled before that branch.

### Step 4: Tests for the HTTP server (`tests/test_local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `tests/test_local_http.py`. Each test starts a `LocalHttpServer` on an ephemeral port (`port=0`), with `materialization_dir=tmp_path / "mat"`, then `urllib.request` against `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`. Use `secrets.token_urlsafe`-derived token from the server.
2. **Cover** (each as its own `test_*` function):
   - `/health` returns 200 with the expected JSON, no `Authorization` header sent.
   - `/ingest` without `Authorization` → 401.
   - `/ingest` with wrong bearer → 401.
   - `/ingest` with valid bearer + `multipart/form-data` (use `email.mime` or hand-rolled multipart since stdlib has no client; alternatively use `requests` since it's already a dep) → 200, returned `path` exists, `size` matches uploaded bytes, file lives under `materialization_dir`.
   - `/cleanup` with `{"path": "/etc/passwd"}` → 400, `/etc/passwd` untouched (just check response code; never actually attempt deletion since the handler rejects pre-`unlink`).
   - `/cleanup` with `{"path": "<materialization_dir>/../escape"}` → 400.
   - `/cleanup` deletes a real materialized file → 204; second call on the same path → 204 (idempotent).
3. **Use** `pytest` fixtures to start/stop the server cleanly per test; assert background thread exits on `shutdown()`.

### Step 5: Tests for `file://` handling and lifecycle gating (`tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py`, `tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Create** `tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py`:
   - `test_download_image_accepts_file_url_inside_materialization_dir`: write a fake file inside `tmp_path`, set `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR=tmp_path` via `monkeypatch`, call `download_image_if_url(f"file://{path}", download_target_dir=tmp_path/"out")`, assert returned string equals the resolved local path and the file was NOT copied.
   - Same shape for `download_video_if_url`.
   - `test_rejects_file_url_outside_materialization_dir`: `tmp_path/"in"` is the mat dir, file lives in `tmp_path/"out"`, expect `DownloadError`.
   - `test_rejects_file_url_with_traversal`: pass `file:///etc/passwd` with mat dir set to a tmp dir → `DownloadError`.
2. **Create** `tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py`:
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_true_with_only_pat`: `monkeypatch.delenv("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY", raising=False)` etc., assert `_is_local_worker_mode() is True`.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_in_cloud`: set `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=...`, assert `False`. Also test `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` alias.
   - This is the smallest verification that the gating logic in `main()` will skip `start_local_http_server` in cloud mode without spinning up the full worker.

### Step 6: Verify the cleanup wiring contract (read-only)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Open** `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts` (sibling repo) to confirm the URL/payload it sends. Brief asserts it POSTs to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/cleanup` with `{"path": ...}` and reads the auth token from `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token`. Cross-check field names and auth header shape against our handler.
2. **If the contract diverges** (e.g. different field name, different file location, different port-discovery mechanism), record the discrepancy in the deliverable summary and adjust handler/file paths to match the app side — the app side already shipped, so we conform to it. **Flag** rather than fix on the app side.

### Step 7: Run the test suite
**Scope:** Small

1. **Run** `pytest tests/test_local_http.py tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py -v` (focused first).
2. **Run** the broader `pytest -x` to confirm no regressions in adjacent modules. Worker tests that import `source.runtime.worker.server` will now also import the helper; make sure the new module has no heavy import side effects (the worker's startup-time imports are already fragile per `server.py` comments).

## Execution Order

1. Land Step 1 (server module) and Step 4 (tests for it) together — the module can be developed and tested in isolation without touching the worker boot path.
2. Land Step 3 (`file://` support) and Step 5's `test_download_utils_file_url.py` together — also isolated, low-risk, fast feedback.
3. Step 2 (lifecycle wiring) and Step 5's lifecycle gating test land last; they touch the long-running worker entrypoint and only pay off once steps 1+3 are green.
4. Step 6 is a read-only cross-check before declaring done.

## Validation Order

1. Targeted unit tests from Step 4 + Step 5 first (cheapest signal, no GPU/wgp imports).
2. Full `pytest` last to confirm we didn't break unrelated suites.
3. Manual trace (see Verification in the brief): boot a PAT-mode worker locally, `curl http://127.0.0.1:<port>/health`, `curl -F file=@/path/to/x.png -H "Authorization: Bearer $(cat ~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token)" http://127.0.0.1:<port>/ingest`, then `/cleanup`. Boot with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=...` set and confirm port 0.0.0.0/127.0.0.1 has nothing listening.

  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 1,
    "idea": "# Local-worker HTTP endpoints + file:// input handling\n\nThis task lives in the **reigh-worker** repo (a Python project that polls a Supabase queue for tasks and executes them on GPU). It's the worker side of the local-file-handle work that already shipped on the reigh-app side.\n\n## Background (why this exists)\n\nreigh-app now has two flows for getting a user's local file (a `FileSystemFileHandle` persisted in the browser) into a worker task as input:\n\n- **Remote worker fallback (already shipped, used today)**: app uploads the bytes to Supabase Storage, puts a public URL into task params, worker downloads from the URL.\n- **Local worker fast path (DORMANT \u2014 what this task enables)**: when the worker is running locally on the same machine as the user's browser, the resolver in `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/{healthcheck,ingest}.ts` is supposed to:\n  1. probe `http://localhost:<port>/health` to detect the worker\n  2. POST file bytes to `http://localhost:<port>/ingest`, receive back `{ path: \"/abs/path/on/disk\" }`\n  3. put `file://<path>` into task params\n  4. after task completion, POST to `http://localhost:<port>/cleanup` with the path so the worker deletes the materialized copy\n\nThe worker today has no HTTP server \u2014 it's a polling client (`source/runtime/worker/server.py` despite its name is not an HTTP server). It also can't read `file://` URLs in most code paths (see #2 below).\n\nThis task is what makes the local fast-path actually work end-to-end.\n\n## What to build\n\n### 1. HTTP server with three endpoints in reigh-worker\n\nAdd a small HTTP server that runs alongside the existing polling worker process. Endpoints:\n\n- `GET /health` \u2014 returns 200 + JSON `{\"ok\": true, \"worker_id\": \"...\", \"version\": \"...\"}`. Cheap, no auth check beyond localhost.\n- `POST /ingest` \u2014 accepts `multipart/form-data` with one file part. Writes the bytes to a known materialization dir (e.g. `~/.reigh-local-files/<uuid>.<ext>`), returns 200 + `{\"path\": \"/abs/path\", \"size\": <bytes>}`. Auth: see below.\n- `POST /cleanup` \u2014 accepts JSON `{\"path\": \"/abs/path\"}`. Validates the path is inside the materialization dir (no escapes), deletes it, returns 204. Auth: see below.\n\n**Auth model**: bind to `127.0.0.1` only (NOT `0.0.0.0`). Even on localhost, an attacker process on the same machine could hit the endpoints \u2014 so require a shared secret on `/ingest` and `/cleanup` via `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header. The token is generated at worker startup, written to a 0600 file at `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (owner-only readable). The reigh-app side reads that file before making requests.\n\n`/health` does NOT require the token (otherwise the probe can't tell \"worker present\" from \"wrong token\"); it leaks zero sensitive info.\n\n**Server framework**: pick whichever is least invasive \u2014 stdlib `http.server` is fine for this scale (<10 req/sec, single user). Avoid pulling in FastAPI/aiohttp unless they're already present in worker deps. Investigate `pyproject.toml` / `requirements.txt` first.\n\n**Lifecycle**: server runs in a background thread spun up at worker startup, dies cleanly on shutdown (SIGINT/SIGTERM). It's PAT-mode-only \u2014 when the worker is launched with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (cloud mode), do NOT start the server. Detection logic exists in `source/runtime/worker/server.py:_resolve_worker_db_client_key()` \u2014 reuse it, don't duplicate.\n\n**Port**: configurable via env var (e.g. `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT`), default to a sensible high port. Write the chosen port to `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` so reigh-app can find it (alongside the auth-token file).\n\n**Materialization dir**: configurable env var (e.g. `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR`), default to `~/.reigh-local-files`. Create with mode 0700 if missing. Hard cap: never write outside this dir; never accept a path component containing `..`.\n\n### 2. file:// support in download_utils.py\n\n`source/utils/download_utils.py` is what the worker uses to fetch task input bytes. Currently it handles `http://` and `https://` URLs by streaming with `requests.get()`. For `file://` URLs (which the new local fast-path produces), it does NOT strip the scheme \u2014 it treats `file:///path/to/foo.jpg` as a literal local path, which fails in most call sites.\n\nFix: in the URL-detection helper (currently around line 205\u2013206), recognize `file://` as a URL scheme. When the scheme is `file`, strip the scheme prefix to get the local path, validate that the path exists and is inside an allowed dir (see materialization dir above \u2014 only allow file:// inputs that point inside that dir), then return the path WITHOUT downloading. Don't copy the file; the worker is allowed to read it in place.\n\nApply this to BOTH `download_image_if_url()` and `download_video_if_url()` (and any siblings). There's apparently a working `file://` handler at `source/media/structure/download.py` \u2014 read it for reference but don't depend on it (the image/video paths are the high-value ones).\n\n### 3. Wire the cleanup hook to actually fire\n\nThe reigh-app side's `complete_task` edge function calls back into the worker's `/cleanup` for each materialized input. Verify that wiring works end-to-end. If the edge function passes the materialized paths through some indirection, make sure the cleanup actually reaches the right worker.\n\nThe edge function code is in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts` (already shipped). Read it to understand the contract \u2014 what URL does it call, what payload does it send, what does it expect back?\n\n## Tests\n\n- `/health` returns 200, no auth.\n- `/ingest` rejects without auth token (401).\n- `/ingest` rejects with wrong token (401).\n- `/ingest` accepts multipart upload, writes file, returns path.\n- `/cleanup` rejects path-traversal (`/etc/passwd`, `../../foo`).\n- `/cleanup` deletes valid file, returns 204.\n- `/cleanup` is idempotent (already-deleted file \u2192 204, not 500).\n- `download_image_if_url` and `download_video_if_url` accept `file://` paths inside materialization dir.\n- Both reject `file://` paths outside materialization dir (security \u2014 don't let task params point at arbitrary local files).\n- Server doesn't start when `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is set (cloud mode).\n- Server starts when only PAT is set (local mode).\n\nUse the worker's existing test framework (likely pytest \u2014 check `pyproject.toml`).\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Don't refactor unrelated code. Don't add new abstractions. Don't add comments explaining the change.\n- Reuse existing helpers \u2014 particularly the auth-mode detection in `source/runtime/worker/server.py`.\n- Don't pull in heavyweight HTTP frameworks. `http.server` from stdlib is sufficient. If you genuinely need something more, justify it in the plan.\n- Keep the server code in a clearly-named module (e.g. `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`).\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- The reigh-app side is already done \u2014 don't touch it unless you find a real bug in `localWorker/{healthcheck,ingest}.ts` that blocks the integration. Flag, don't fix.\n- Don't add metrics, logging frameworks, prometheus, etc. Plain `print()` or whatever the worker already uses is fine.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Run the test suite (`pytest` or whatever the project uses).\n- Manually trace the auth flow end-to-end: server starts \u2192 token + port written \u2192 reigh-app can read both \u2192 `/health` reachable \u2192 `/ingest` accepts an authed upload \u2192 `/cleanup` removes it.\n- Confirm cloud-mode launch (with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`) does not start the server.\n\n## Deliverables\n\n- New module(s) for the HTTP server, with tests.\n- `download_utils.py` updated for `file://` with tests.\n- Lifecycle integration into worker startup/shutdown.\n- A short note in your final summary describing: which port + dir defaults you chose, where the auth token + port file live on disk, any places the reigh-app side will need a small follow-up tweak.",
