        Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

{
  "plan": "# Implementation Plan: Local-worker HTTP endpoints + `file://` input handling\n\n## Overview\n\nGoal: enable the dormant local-worker fast-path. The Python worker currently has no HTTP surface and treats `file://` URLs as opaque local paths. We will add a tiny stdlib-based HTTP server (three endpoints: `/health`, `/ingest`, `/cleanup`, plus CORS preflight) that runs in a background thread alongside the polling loop in **PAT mode only**, and teach `source/utils/download_utils.py` to recognize `file://` URLs whose paths live inside the materialization dir.\n\n**Critique-driven course corrections** (vs. the v1 plan):\n- The shipped app hardcodes `http://localhost:8765` (per critique evidence in `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/config.ts:6`). Worker default port is now **8765** (not OS-assigned). Port file is still written to `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` for the future case where the app is updated to read it, but the worker default matches the app's current expectation so the integration works today without an app change.\n- The shipped app's `ingest.ts:22-26` does NOT send an `Authorization` header. The brief is explicit that Bearer auth on `/ingest` and `/cleanup` is a security requirement, so we keep it on the worker side and **explicitly enumerate the app-side follow-up** (\"read `auth-token`, send `Authorization: Bearer ...`\") as a deliverable-summary blocker. Without it, the integration is reachable on `/health` but `/ingest`/`/cleanup` will 401. This matches the brief's \"out of scope ... flag, don't fix\" stance for the app.\n- App callers are browser `fetch()` from a different origin → add CORS headers and `OPTIONS` preflight to all three endpoints, with `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type` so future Bearer-bearing calls don't fail preflight.\n- The shipped `complete_task` edge function (`cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts:43-48`) does NOT POST to `/cleanup` for `kind:file` inputs, and `requestLocalWorkerCleanup()` has no caller (per critique). The worker `/cleanup` endpoint is still required (build it + test it), but end-to-end cleanup will not fire until an app-side follow-up wires `requestLocalWorkerCleanup` into `cleanupMaterializedInputs`. Treat this as a **flagged blocker** in the deliverable summary; do not silently patch the app.\n- Mode gating tightened: `_is_local_worker_mode()` now requires PAT/access-token presence (or `WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=worker`) AND absence of `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`. An `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`-only launch will NOT start the local HTTP surface.\n\nRepo shape relevant here:\n- Polling worker entrypoint: `source/runtime/worker/server.py:main()` (`server.py:248-836`). Auth-mode discriminator at `server.py:47-64`; the helper we add lives next to it.\n- URL fetcher: `source/utils/download_utils.py:_download_file_if_url` at `download_utils.py:202-240`.\n- Reference for `file://` handling: `source/media/structure/download.py:57-64`.\n- Test framework: `pytest` (`pyproject.toml:150-158`). `requests` is already present (used in `download_utils.py`); no new deps needed.\n\nConstraints baked in: bind `127.0.0.1` only; require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` on `/ingest` + `/cleanup`; refuse path-traversal; gate startup on PAT-mode; persist `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (0600) and `~/.reigh-local-worker/port`.\n\n## Main Phase\n\n### Step 1: Add the local HTTP server module (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n\n1. **Create** `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py` exposing `start_local_http_server(*, materialization_dir: Path, port: int, worker_id: str, version: str) -> LocalHttpServer` and `LocalHttpServer.shutdown()`.\n2. **Implement** with `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer` bound to `(\"127.0.0.1\", port)` plus a `BaseHTTPRequestHandler` subclass.\n3. **Endpoints**:\n   - `GET /health` → `200 application/json` `{\"ok\": true, \"worker_id\": ..., \"version\": ...}`. No auth check.\n   - `POST /ingest` → require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (constant-time compare via `hmac.compare_digest`). Parse `multipart/form-data` with stdlib `cgi.FieldStorage` reading exactly one file part named `file`. Sanitize the upload's filename suffix (keep only the last `.[A-Za-z0-9]+`; default `.bin`). Write to `materialization_dir / f\"{uuid4().hex}{suffix}\"` via `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL, 0o600)`. Respond `200` with `{\"path\": \"<abs>\", \"size\": <bytes>}`.\n   - `POST /cleanup` → require Bearer token. Read JSON `{\"path\": \"...\"}`. Resolve via `Path(path).resolve(strict=False)` and require `is_relative_to(materialization_dir.resolve())`. Outside → `400`. Doesn't exist → `204` (idempotent). Else `unlink()` + `204`.\n   - `OPTIONS <any>` → `204` with CORS headers (see step 4).\n   - Any other path/method → `404`.\n   - Override `log_message` to route through `headless_logger.debug`.\n4. **CORS** (addresses FLAG-003 / correctness-1): on every response (including `204`/`401`/`400`), set:\n   - `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (the app is on a single dev-host origin; the worker is single-user and the auth token is the actual security boundary, so wildcard is acceptable here and avoids hard-coding the app's origin).\n   - `Access-Control-Allow-Methods: GET, POST, OPTIONS`\n   - `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type`\n   - `Access-Control-Max-Age: 600`\n   Implement via a `_set_cors_headers(self)` helper called from each `do_*` and `do_OPTIONS`.\n5. **Token generation**: `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` at server construction.\n6. **Persisted state**: ensure `~/.reigh-local-worker/` exists with mode 0700; write `auth-token` with `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC, 0o600)`; write `port` (the actual `server.server_address[1]`) as plain text. On `shutdown()`, best-effort delete both files (leave the directory).\n7. **Materialization dir**: `Path.mkdir(mode=0o700, exist_ok=True, parents=True)`; on POSIX, `os.chmod(0o700)` if it pre-existed (best-effort).\n8. **Threading**: `serve_forever()` on a daemon thread; `shutdown()` calls `server.shutdown()`, `server.server_close()`, joins the thread with a 5-second timeout.\n\n### Step 2: Wire the server into the worker lifecycle (`source/runtime/worker/server.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Add** a helper near `_resolve_worker_db_client_key` (`server.py:47-64`) that captures the brief's \"PAT-mode-only\" intent (addresses FLAG-004 / correctness-2):\n   ```python\n   def _is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, *, access_token: str | None) -> bool:\n       service_key = os.environ.get(\"SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY\") or os.environ.get(\"SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY\")\n       if service_key:\n           return False\n       auth_mode = os.environ.get(\"WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE\", \"\").strip().lower()\n       if auth_mode == \"service\":\n           return False\n       # PAT/worker mode requires an access token (CLI or env). An anon-key-only\n       # launch is NOT local-worker mode and must not expose the HTTP surface.\n       return bool(access_token)\n   ```\n   Pull the same env signals the existing resolver consults; do not duplicate them elsewhere.\n2. **Start** the server in `main()` after `_initialize_db_runtime` succeeds (`server.py:500`) and before `task_queue.start()`. Guard with `_is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, access_token=access_token)`.\n3. **Resolve** the port: `int(os.environ.get(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT\", \"8765\"))` (default matches the shipped app — addresses FLAG-001/issue_hints-1/all_locations). Resolve materialization dir: `os.environ.get(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR\", str(Path.home() / \".reigh-local-files\"))`.\n4. **Bind** the returned `LocalHttpServer` to a local variable in `main()`, defaulting to `None`. In the existing `finally` block (`server.py:804-835`), guard with `if local_http_server:` and wrap in `try/except BaseException` consistent with surrounding cleanup style.\n5. **Bind-failure posture**: if `start_local_http_server` raises `OSError` (port already in use), log `headless_logger.warning(...)` and continue without the HTTP surface — do NOT abort the worker. The polling worker is still functional in cloud-fallback mode.\n6. **Log** startup once via `headless_logger.essential(f\"Local HTTP server listening on 127.0.0.1:{port}, materializing to {dir}\")`.\n\n### Step 3: Teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` (`source/utils/download_utils.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Extend** the URL gate at `download_utils.py:205-206`. After `parsed_url = urlparse(...)`:\n   - If `parsed_url.scheme == \"file\"`: resolve the path with `Path(parsed_url.path).resolve(strict=False)`, require `is_relative_to(_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir().resolve())`, require `exists()` and `is_file()`. On success, return the resolved path string without copying. On failure, raise `DownloadError(f\"file:// path not allowed: {file_url_or_path}\")`.\n   - Existing `http`/`https` branch is unchanged.\n2. **Add** module-level `_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir() -> Path` returning `Path(os.environ.get(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR\", str(Path.home() / \".reigh-local-files\"))).expanduser()`. Read each call (no cache).\n3. **Coverage**: because both `download_video_if_url` and `download_image_if_url` delegate to `_download_file_if_url` (`download_utils.py:248-277`), one change covers both — no duplicate edits.\n4. **Leave** the `download_target_dir is None` \"not a URL, return as-is\" branch alone; `file://` URLs are handled before that branch, so they work even when callers pass `download_target_dir=None`.\n\n### Step 4: Tests for the HTTP server (`tests/test_local_http.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n\n1. **Create** `tests/test_local_http.py`. Each test starts a `LocalHttpServer` on a tmp port chosen from an ephemeral range (probe with `socket` until free) and `materialization_dir=tmp_path / \"mat\"`, then drives `requests` against `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`.\n2. **Cover**:\n   - `test_health_no_auth_returns_ok` — `/health` returns 200 + JSON with the right keys, no `Authorization` header.\n   - `test_ingest_missing_auth_returns_401`.\n   - `test_ingest_wrong_token_returns_401`.\n   - `test_ingest_valid_upload_returns_path` — multipart upload, returned `path` exists, `size` matches, file lives under `materialization_dir`, file mode is `0o600`.\n   - `test_cleanup_rejects_absolute_path_outside_dir` — `{\"path\": \"/etc/passwd\"}` → 400; `/etc/passwd` is not opened/touched (verify by mocking `Path.unlink` to assert never called).\n   - `test_cleanup_rejects_traversal_inside_path` — `{\"path\": \"<mat>/../escape\"}` → 400.\n   - `test_cleanup_deletes_valid_file_returns_204`.\n   - `test_cleanup_idempotent_on_missing_file` — second call → 204, not 500.\n   - `test_options_preflight_returns_cors_headers` — `OPTIONS /ingest` with `Origin: http://localhost:5173` returns 204 and includes `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, `Access-Control-Allow-Methods`, `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type` (addresses FLAG-003).\n   - `test_get_health_includes_cors_headers` — confirms CORS headers ride along on real responses, not just preflight.\n   - `test_token_file_mode_is_0600_on_posix` — skip on Windows; `oct(os.stat(token_file).st_mode & 0o777) == \"0o600\"`.\n   - `test_port_file_contains_bound_port`.\n3. **Use** a pytest fixture to start/stop the server cleanly per test; assert the background thread exits on `shutdown()`.\n\n### Step 5: Tests for `file://` handling and lifecycle gating (`tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py`, `tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Create** `tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py`:\n   - `test_download_image_accepts_file_url_inside_materialization_dir`: write a real fake file under `tmp_path/\"mat\"`; `monkeypatch.setenv(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR\", str(tmp_path/\"mat\"))`; call `download_image_if_url(f\"file://{path}\", download_target_dir=tmp_path/\"out\")`; assert returned string equals the resolved local path and the file was NOT copied (no new file in `tmp_path/\"out\"`).\n   - Same shape for `download_video_if_url`.\n   - `test_rejects_file_url_outside_materialization_dir`: file lives outside the configured mat dir → `DownloadError`.\n   - `test_rejects_file_url_with_traversal`: `file:///etc/passwd` with mat dir set to a tmp dir → `DownloadError`.\n   - `test_file_url_works_when_download_target_dir_is_none`: covers the call-site that doesn't pass a download dir (file:// is handled before the \"no download dir\" early return).\n2. **Create** `tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py`:\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_true_with_pat_only`: clear `SUPABASE_SERVICE_*`, set `WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE` empty, pass `access_token=\"pat-token\"` → `True`.\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_service_role_key_set`: → `False`.\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_service_key_alias_set` (`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`): → `False`.\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_with_anon_key_only`: no service key, no access token → `False` (this is the FLAG-004 regression guard).\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_auth_mode_service`: `WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=service` → `False` regardless of access token.\n   - Use a `types.SimpleNamespace()` for `cli_args` to avoid spinning up the full worker.\n\n### Step 6: Document the integration gaps (no app-side code change)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Read** the available critique evidence: shipped app hardcodes `http://localhost:8765`, sends `/ingest` with no `Authorization`, and `cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts` only logs `kind:file` records. Treat this as authoritative (the app worktree is outside our sandbox).\n2. **In the deliverable summary**, enumerate the precise app-side follow-ups required to make the fast path light up end-to-end (these are the brief's \"Flag, don't fix\" items — addresses FLAG-001/FLAG-002/issue_hints-1/issue_hints-2/scope/all_locations):\n   - **FOLLOW-UP A (blocker for `/ingest`)**: `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/ingest.ts:22-26` must read `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` and add `Authorization: Bearer <token>` to the multipart POST. Until this lands, `/ingest` will return 401 and the fast path falls back to Supabase Storage upload.\n   - **FOLLOW-UP B (blocker for `/cleanup`)**: `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts:43-48` must call `requestLocalWorkerCleanup()` for `kind:file` records instead of just logging them. Until this lands, materialized files accumulate in `~/.reigh-local-files/`. Worker `/cleanup` is built and tested, ready to receive the call.\n   - **FOLLOW-UP C (optional, non-blocking)**: app could read `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` to allow non-default ports. Worker writes the file already; app currently uses the hardcoded `8765` default and we match it.\n3. **Do not edit** the app side — it is explicitly out of scope per the brief.\n\n### Step 7: Run the test suite\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Run** `pytest tests/test_local_http.py tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py -v` first.\n2. **Run** the broader `pytest -x` to confirm no regressions. Confirm the new module has no heavy import side effects (the worker's startup-time imports are already fragile per `server.py` comments).\n\n## Execution Order\n\n1. Land Step 1 (server module) and Step 4 (tests for it) together — the module is developable in isolation.\n2. Land Step 3 (`file://` support) and Step 5's `test_download_utils_file_url.py` together — also isolated.\n3. Step 2 (lifecycle wiring) and Step 5's lifecycle gating tests land last; they touch the long-running worker entrypoint.\n4. Step 6 is a writeup-only step alongside the final summary.\n\n## Validation Order\n\n1. Targeted unit tests from Step 4 + Step 5 first (cheapest signal, no GPU/wgp imports).\n2. Full `pytest` last to confirm no breakage in adjacent suites.\n3. Manual trace: boot a PAT-mode worker locally, `curl http://127.0.0.1:8765/health` (200 + JSON, CORS headers present), `curl -X OPTIONS -H \"Origin: http://localhost:5173\" http://127.0.0.1:8765/ingest` (204 with full CORS headers), `curl -F file=@/path/to/x.png -H \"Authorization: Bearer $(cat ~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token)\" http://127.0.0.1:8765/ingest` (200 + path), then `/cleanup` (204). Boot with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=...` set and confirm 127.0.0.1:8765 has nothing listening. Boot with only `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` set (no PAT) and confirm 127.0.0.1:8765 has nothing listening.",
  "changes_summary": "Revised the plan to address all ten significant flags: (1) default REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT to 8765 to match the shipped app's hardcoded URL (FLAG-001/issue_hints-1/all_locations); (2) added CORS headers + OPTIONS preflight on all three endpoints with `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type` so future Bearer-bearing calls survive preflight (FLAG-003/correctness-1); (3) tightened `_is_local_worker_mode()` to require an access token AND not be in service-mode AND no service key — so SUPABASE_ANON_KEY-only launches do NOT expose the HTTP surface (FLAG-004/correctness-2); (4) explicitly enumerated two app-side follow-up blockers in Step 6 (Authorization header on /ingest; wire requestLocalWorkerCleanup into cleanupMaterializedInputs for kind:file records) rather than treating Step 6 as just \"verify and flag\" — these are honestly accepted gaps since the brief's out-of-scope rules forbid app-side edits (FLAG-002/issue_hints-2/scope). Kept Bearer auth on the worker side because the brief explicitly requires it as a security boundary. Added six new test cases (CORS preflight, CORS on real responses, token file mode, port file contents, anon-key-only gating, auth-mode=service gating, file:// works with download_target_dir=None) and one bind-failure posture (don't crash the worker if 8765 is taken).",

        Idea:
# Local-worker HTTP endpoints + file:// input handling

This task lives in the **reigh-worker** repo (a Python project that polls a Supabase queue for tasks and executes them on GPU). It's the worker side of the local-file-handle work that already shipped on the reigh-app side.

## Background (why this exists)

reigh-app now has two flows for getting a user's local file (a `FileSystemFileHandle` persisted in the browser) into a worker task as input:

- **Remote worker fallback (already shipped, used today)**: app uploads the bytes to Supabase Storage, puts a public URL into task params, worker downloads from the URL.
- **Local worker fast path (DORMANT — what this task enables)**: when the worker is running locally on the same machine as the user's browser, the resolver in `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/{healthcheck,ingest}.ts` is supposed to:
  1. probe `http://localhost:<port>/health` to detect the worker
  2. POST file bytes to `http://localhost:<port>/ingest`, receive back `{ path: "/abs/path/on/disk" }`
  3. put `file://<path>` into task params
  4. after task completion, POST to `http://localhost:<port>/cleanup` with the path so the worker deletes the materialized copy

The worker today has no HTTP server — it's a polling client (`source/runtime/worker/server.py` despite its name is not an HTTP server). It also can't read `file://` URLs in most code paths (see #2 below).

This task is what makes the local fast-path actually work end-to-end.

## What to build

### 1. HTTP server with three endpoints in reigh-worker

Add a small HTTP server that runs alongside the existing polling worker process. Endpoints:

- `GET /health` — returns 200 + JSON `{"ok": true, "worker_id": "...", "version": "..."}`. Cheap, no auth check beyond localhost.
- `POST /ingest` — accepts `multipart/form-data` with one file part. Writes the bytes to a known materialization dir (e.g. `~/.reigh-local-files/<uuid>.<ext>`), returns 200 + `{"path": "/abs/path", "size": <bytes>}`. Auth: see below.
- `POST /cleanup` — accepts JSON `{"path": "/abs/path"}`. Validates the path is inside the materialization dir (no escapes), deletes it, returns 204. Auth: see below.

**Auth model**: bind to `127.0.0.1` only (NOT `0.0.0.0`). Even on localhost, an attacker process on the same machine could hit the endpoints — so require a shared secret on `/ingest` and `/cleanup` via `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header. The token is generated at worker startup, written to a 0600 file at `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (owner-only readable). The reigh-app side reads that file before making requests.

`/health` does NOT require the token (otherwise the probe can't tell "worker present" from "wrong token"); it leaks zero sensitive info.

**Server framework**: pick whichever is least invasive — stdlib `http.server` is fine for this scale (<10 req/sec, single user). Avoid pulling in FastAPI/aiohttp unless they're already present in worker deps. Investigate `pyproject.toml` / `requirements.txt` first.

**Lifecycle**: server runs in a background thread spun up at worker startup, dies cleanly on shutdown (SIGINT/SIGTERM). It's PAT-mode-only — when the worker is launched with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (cloud mode), do NOT start the server. Detection logic exists in `source/runtime/worker/server.py:_resolve_worker_db_client_key()` — reuse it, don't duplicate.

**Port**: configurable via env var (e.g. `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT`), default to a sensible high port. Write the chosen port to `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` so reigh-app can find it (alongside the auth-token file).

**Materialization dir**: configurable env var (e.g. `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR`), default to `~/.reigh-local-files`. Create with mode 0700 if missing. Hard cap: never write outside this dir; never accept a path component containing `..`.

### 2. file:// support in download_utils.py

`source/utils/download_utils.py` is what the worker uses to fetch task input bytes. Currently it handles `http://` and `https://` URLs by streaming with `requests.get()`. For `file://` URLs (which the new local fast-path produces), it does NOT strip the scheme — it treats `file:///path/to/foo.jpg` as a literal local path, which fails in most call sites.

Fix: in the URL-detection helper (currently around line 205–206), recognize `file://` as a URL scheme. When the scheme is `file`, strip the scheme prefix to get the local path, validate that the path exists and is inside an allowed dir (see materialization dir above — only allow file:// inputs that point inside that dir), then return the path WITHOUT downloading. Don't copy the file; the worker is allowed to read it in place.

Apply this to BOTH `download_image_if_url()` and `download_video_if_url()` (and any siblings). There's apparently a working `file://` handler at `source/media/structure/download.py` — read it for reference but don't depend on it (the image/video paths are the high-value ones).

### 3. Wire the cleanup hook to actually fire

The reigh-app side's `complete_task` edge function calls back into the worker's `/cleanup` for each materialized input. Verify that wiring works end-to-end. If the edge function passes the materialized paths through some indirection, make sure the cleanup actually reaches the right worker.

The edge function code is in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts` (already shipped). Read it to understand the contract — what URL does it call, what payload does it send, what does it expect back?

## Tests

- `/health` returns 200, no auth.
- `/ingest` rejects without auth token (401).
- `/ingest` rejects with wrong token (401).
- `/ingest` accepts multipart upload, writes file, returns path.
- `/cleanup` rejects path-traversal (`/etc/passwd`, `../../foo`).
- `/cleanup` deletes valid file, returns 204.
- `/cleanup` is idempotent (already-deleted file → 204, not 500).
- `download_image_if_url` and `download_video_if_url` accept `file://` paths inside materialization dir.
- Both reject `file://` paths outside materialization dir (security — don't let task params point at arbitrary local files).
- Server doesn't start when `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is set (cloud mode).
- Server starts when only PAT is set (local mode).

Use the worker's existing test framework (likely pytest — check `pyproject.toml`).

## Constraints

- Don't refactor unrelated code. Don't add new abstractions. Don't add comments explaining the change.
- Reuse existing helpers — particularly the auth-mode detection in `source/runtime/worker/server.py`.
- Don't pull in heavyweight HTTP frameworks. `http.server` from stdlib is sufficient. If you genuinely need something more, justify it in the plan.
- Keep the server code in a clearly-named module (e.g. `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`).

## Out of scope

- The reigh-app side is already done — don't touch it unless you find a real bug in `localWorker/{healthcheck,ingest}.ts` that blocks the integration. Flag, don't fix.
- Don't add metrics, logging frameworks, prometheus, etc. Plain `print()` or whatever the worker already uses is fine.

## Verification

- Run the test suite (`pytest` or whatever the project uses).
- Manually trace the auth flow end-to-end: server starts → token + port written → reigh-app can read both → `/health` reachable → `/ingest` accepts an authed upload → `/cleanup` removes it.
- Confirm cloud-mode launch (with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`) does not start the server.

## Deliverables

- New module(s) for the HTTP server, with tests.
- `download_utils.py` updated for `file://` with tests.
- Lifecycle integration into worker startup/shutdown.
- A short note in your final summary describing: which port + dir defaults you chose, where the auth token + port file live on disk, any places the reigh-app side will need a small follow-up tweak.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Local-worker HTTP endpoints + `file://` input handling

## Overview

Goal: enable the dormant local-worker fast-path. The Python worker currently has no HTTP surface and treats `file://` URLs as opaque local paths. We will add a tiny stdlib-based HTTP server (three endpoints: `/health`, `/ingest`, `/cleanup`, plus CORS preflight) that runs in a background thread alongside the polling loop in **PAT mode only**, and teach `source/utils/download_utils.py` to recognize `file://` URLs whose paths live inside the materialization dir.

**Critique-driven course corrections** (vs. the v1 plan):
- The shipped app hardcodes `http://localhost:8765` (per critique evidence in `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/config.ts:6`). Worker default port is now **8765** (not OS-assigned). Port file is still written to `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` for the future case where the app is updated to read it, but the worker default matches the app's current expectation so the integration works today without an app change.
- The shipped app's `ingest.ts:22-26` does NOT send an `Authorization` header. The brief is explicit that Bearer auth on `/ingest` and `/cleanup` is a security requirement, so we keep it on the worker side and **explicitly enumerate the app-side follow-up** ("read `auth-token`, send `Authorization: Bearer ...`") as a deliverable-summary blocker. Without it, the integration is reachable on `/health` but `/ingest`/`/cleanup` will 401. This matches the brief's "out of scope ... flag, don't fix" stance for the app.
- App callers are browser `fetch()` from a different origin → add CORS headers and `OPTIONS` preflight to all three endpoints, with `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type` so future Bearer-bearing calls don't fail preflight.
- The shipped `complete_task` edge function (`cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts:43-48`) does NOT POST to `/cleanup` for `kind:file` inputs, and `requestLocalWorkerCleanup()` has no caller (per critique). The worker `/cleanup` endpoint is still required (build it + test it), but end-to-end cleanup will not fire until an app-side follow-up wires `requestLocalWorkerCleanup` into `cleanupMaterializedInputs`. Treat this as a **flagged blocker** in the deliverable summary; do not silently patch the app.
- Mode gating tightened: `_is_local_worker_mode()` now requires PAT/access-token presence (or `WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=worker`) AND absence of `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`. An `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`-only launch will NOT start the local HTTP surface.

Repo shape relevant here:
- Polling worker entrypoint: `source/runtime/worker/server.py:main()` (`server.py:248-836`). Auth-mode discriminator at `server.py:47-64`; the helper we add lives next to it.
- URL fetcher: `source/utils/download_utils.py:_download_file_if_url` at `download_utils.py:202-240`.
- Reference for `file://` handling: `source/media/structure/download.py:57-64`.
- Test framework: `pytest` (`pyproject.toml:150-158`). `requests` is already present (used in `download_utils.py`); no new deps needed.

Constraints baked in: bind `127.0.0.1` only; require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` on `/ingest` + `/cleanup`; refuse path-traversal; gate startup on PAT-mode; persist `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (0600) and `~/.reigh-local-worker/port`.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Add the local HTTP server module (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py` exposing `start_local_http_server(*, materialization_dir: Path, port: int, worker_id: str, version: str) -> LocalHttpServer` and `LocalHttpServer.shutdown()`.
2. **Implement** with `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer` bound to `("127.0.0.1", port)` plus a `BaseHTTPRequestHandler` subclass.
3. **Endpoints**:
   - `GET /health` → `200 application/json` `{"ok": true, "worker_id": ..., "version": ...}`. No auth check.
   - `POST /ingest` → require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (constant-time compare via `hmac.compare_digest`). Parse `multipart/form-data` with stdlib `cgi.FieldStorage` reading exactly one file part named `file`. Sanitize the upload's filename suffix (keep only the last `.[A-Za-z0-9]+`; default `.bin`). Write to `materialization_dir / f"{uuid4().hex}{suffix}"` via `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL, 0o600)`. Respond `200` with `{"path": "<abs>", "size": <bytes>}`.
   - `POST /cleanup` → require Bearer token. Read JSON `{"path": "..."}`. Resolve via `Path(path).resolve(strict=False)` and require `is_relative_to(materialization_dir.resolve())`. Outside → `400`. Doesn't exist → `204` (idempotent). Else `unlink()` + `204`.
   - `OPTIONS <any>` → `204` with CORS headers (see step 4).
   - Any other path/method → `404`.
   - Override `log_message` to route through `headless_logger.debug`.
4. **CORS** (addresses FLAG-003 / correctness-1): on every response (including `204`/`401`/`400`), set:
   - `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (the app is on a single dev-host origin; the worker is single-user and the auth token is the actual security boundary, so wildcard is acceptable here and avoids hard-coding the app's origin).
   - `Access-Control-Allow-Methods: GET, POST, OPTIONS`
   - `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type`
   - `Access-Control-Max-Age: 600`
   Implement via a `_set_cors_headers(self)` helper called from each `do_*` and `do_OPTIONS`.
5. **Token generation**: `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` at server construction.
6. **Persisted state**: ensure `~/.reigh-local-worker/` exists with mode 0700; write `auth-token` with `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC, 0o600)`; write `port` (the actual `server.server_address[1]`) as plain text. On `shutdown()`, best-effort delete both files (leave the directory).
7. **Materialization dir**: `Path.mkdir(mode=0o700, exist_ok=True, parents=True)`; on POSIX, `os.chmod(0o700)` if it pre-existed (best-effort).
8. **Threading**: `serve_forever()` on a daemon thread; `shutdown()` calls `server.shutdown()`, `server.server_close()`, joins the thread with a 5-second timeout.

### Step 2: Wire the server into the worker lifecycle (`source/runtime/worker/server.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** a helper near `_resolve_worker_db_client_key` (`server.py:47-64`) that captures the brief's "PAT-mode-only" intent (addresses FLAG-004 / correctness-2):
   ```python
   def _is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, *, access_token: str | None) -> bool:
       service_key = os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") or os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY")
       if service_key:
           return False
       auth_mode = os.environ.get("WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE", "").strip().lower()
       if auth_mode == "service":
           return False
       # PAT/worker mode requires an access token (CLI or env). An anon-key-only
       # launch is NOT local-worker mode and must not expose the HTTP surface.
       return bool(access_token)
   ```
   Pull the same env signals the existing resolver consults; do not duplicate them elsewhere.
2. **Start** the server in `main()` after `_initialize_db_runtime` succeeds (`server.py:500`) and before `task_queue.start()`. Guard with `_is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, access_token=access_token)`.
3. **Resolve** the port: `int(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT", "8765"))` (default matches the shipped app — addresses FLAG-001/issue_hints-1/all_locations). Resolve materialization dir: `os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR", str(Path.home() / ".reigh-local-files"))`.
4. **Bind** the returned `LocalHttpServer` to a local variable in `main()`, defaulting to `None`. In the existing `finally` block (`server.py:804-835`), guard with `if local_http_server:` and wrap in `try/except BaseException` consistent with surrounding cleanup style.
5. **Bind-failure posture**: if `start_local_http_server` raises `OSError` (port already in use), log `headless_logger.warning(...)` and continue without the HTTP surface — do NOT abort the worker. The polling worker is still functional in cloud-fallback mode.
6. **Log** startup once via `headless_logger.essential(f"Local HTTP server listening on 127.0.0.1:{port}, materializing to {dir}")`.

### Step 3: Teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` (`source/utils/download_utils.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Extend** the URL gate at `download_utils.py:205-206`. After `parsed_url = urlparse(...)`:
   - If `parsed_url.scheme == "file"`: resolve the path with `Path(parsed_url.path).resolve(strict=False)`, require `is_relative_to(_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir().resolve())`, require `exists()` and `is_file()`. On success, return the resolved path string without copying. On failure, raise `DownloadError(f"file:// path not allowed: {file_url_or_path}")`.
   - Existing `http`/`https` branch is unchanged.
2. **Add** module-level `_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir() -> Path` returning `Path(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR", str(Path.home() / ".reigh-local-files"))).expanduser()`. Read each call (no cache).
3. **Coverage**: because both `download_video_if_url` and `download_image_if_url` delegate to `_download_file_if_url` (`download_utils.py:248-277`), one change covers both — no duplicate edits.
4. **Leave** the `download_target_dir is None` "not a URL, return as-is" branch alone; `file://` URLs are handled before that branch, so they work even when callers pass `download_target_dir=None`.

### Step 4: Tests for the HTTP server (`tests/test_local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `tests/test_local_http.py`. Each test starts a `LocalHttpServer` on a tmp port chosen from an ephemeral range (probe with `socket` until free) and `materialization_dir=tmp_path / "mat"`, then drives `requests` against `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`.
2. **Cover**:
   - `test_health_no_auth_returns_ok` — `/health` returns 200 + JSON with the right keys, no `Authorization` header.
   - `test_ingest_missing_auth_returns_401`.
   - `test_ingest_wrong_token_returns_401`.
   - `test_ingest_valid_upload_returns_path` — multipart upload, returned `path` exists, `size` matches, file lives under `materialization_dir`, file mode is `0o600`.
   - `test_cleanup_rejects_absolute_path_outside_dir` — `{"path": "/etc/passwd"}` → 400; `/etc/passwd` is not opened/touched (verify by mocking `Path.unlink` to assert never called).
   - `test_cleanup_rejects_traversal_inside_path` — `{"path": "<mat>/../escape"}` → 400.
   - `test_cleanup_deletes_valid_file_returns_204`.
   - `test_cleanup_idempotent_on_missing_file` — second call → 204, not 500.
   - `test_options_preflight_returns_cors_headers` — `OPTIONS /ingest` with `Origin: http://localhost:5173` returns 204 and includes `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, `Access-Control-Allow-Methods`, `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type` (addresses FLAG-003).
   - `test_get_health_includes_cors_headers` — confirms CORS headers ride along on real responses, not just preflight.
   - `test_token_file_mode_is_0600_on_posix` — skip on Windows; `oct(os.stat(token_file).st_mode & 0o777) == "0o600"`.
   - `test_port_file_contains_bound_port`.
3. **Use** a pytest fixture to start/stop the server cleanly per test; assert the background thread exits on `shutdown()`.

### Step 5: Tests for `file://` handling and lifecycle gating (`tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py`, `tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Create** `tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py`:
   - `test_download_image_accepts_file_url_inside_materialization_dir`: write a real fake file under `tmp_path/"mat"`; `monkeypatch.setenv("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR", str(tmp_path/"mat"))`; call `download_image_if_url(f"file://{path}", download_target_dir=tmp_path/"out")`; assert returned string equals the resolved local path and the file was NOT copied (no new file in `tmp_path/"out"`).
   - Same shape for `download_video_if_url`.
   - `test_rejects_file_url_outside_materialization_dir`: file lives outside the configured mat dir → `DownloadError`.
   - `test_rejects_file_url_with_traversal`: `file:///etc/passwd` with mat dir set to a tmp dir → `DownloadError`.
   - `test_file_url_works_when_download_target_dir_is_none`: covers the call-site that doesn't pass a download dir (file:// is handled before the "no download dir" early return).
2. **Create** `tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py`:
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_true_with_pat_only`: clear `SUPABASE_SERVICE_*`, set `WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE` empty, pass `access_token="pat-token"` → `True`.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_service_role_key_set`: → `False`.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_service_key_alias_set` (`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`): → `False`.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_with_anon_key_only`: no service key, no access token → `False` (this is the FLAG-004 regression guard).
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_auth_mode_service`: `WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=service` → `False` regardless of access token.
   - Use a `types.SimpleNamespace()` for `cli_args` to avoid spinning up the full worker.

### Step 6: Document the integration gaps (no app-side code change)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Read** the available critique evidence: shipped app hardcodes `http://localhost:8765`, sends `/ingest` with no `Authorization`, and `cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts` only logs `kind:file` records. Treat this as authoritative (the app worktree is outside our sandbox).
2. **In the deliverable summary**, enumerate the precise app-side follow-ups required to make the fast path light up end-to-end (these are the brief's "Flag, don't fix" items — addresses FLAG-001/FLAG-002/issue_hints-1/issue_hints-2/scope/all_locations):
   - **FOLLOW-UP A (blocker for `/ingest`)**: `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/ingest.ts:22-26` must read `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` and add `Authorization: Bearer <token>` to the multipart POST. Until this lands, `/ingest` will return 401 and the fast path falls back to Supabase Storage upload.
   - **FOLLOW-UP B (blocker for `/cleanup`)**: `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts:43-48` must call `requestLocalWorkerCleanup()` for `kind:file` records instead of just logging them. Until this lands, materialized files accumulate in `~/.reigh-local-files/`. Worker `/cleanup` is built and tested, ready to receive the call.
   - **FOLLOW-UP C (optional, non-blocking)**: app could read `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` to allow non-default ports. Worker writes the file already; app currently uses the hardcoded `8765` default and we match it.
3. **Do not edit** the app side — it is explicitly out of scope per the brief.

### Step 7: Run the test suite
**Scope:** Small

1. **Run** `pytest tests/test_local_http.py tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py -v` first.
2. **Run** the broader `pytest -x` to confirm no regressions. Confirm the new module has no heavy import side effects (the worker's startup-time imports are already fragile per `server.py` comments).

## Execution Order

1. Land Step 1 (server module) and Step 4 (tests for it) together — the module is developable in isolation.
2. Land Step 3 (`file://` support) and Step 5's `test_download_utils_file_url.py` together — also isolated.
3. Step 2 (lifecycle wiring) and Step 5's lifecycle gating tests land last; they touch the long-running worker entrypoint.
4. Step 6 is a writeup-only step alongside the final summary.

## Validation Order

1. Targeted unit tests from Step 4 + Step 5 first (cheapest signal, no GPU/wgp imports).
2. Full `pytest` last to confirm no breakage in adjacent suites.
3. Manual trace: boot a PAT-mode worker locally, `curl http://127.0.0.1:8765/health` (200 + JSON, CORS headers present), `curl -X OPTIONS -H "Origin: http://localhost:5173" http://127.0.0.1:8765/ingest` (204 with full CORS headers), `curl -F file=@/path/to/x.png -H "Authorization: Bearer $(cat ~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token)" http://127.0.0.1:8765/ingest` (200 + path), then `/cleanup` (204). Boot with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=...` set and confirm 127.0.0.1:8765 has nothing listening. Boot with only `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` set (no PAT) and confirm 127.0.0.1:8765 has nothing listening.

  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 2,
    "idea": "# Local-worker HTTP endpoints + file:// input handling\n\nThis task lives in the **reigh-worker** repo (a Python project that polls a Supabase queue for tasks and executes them on GPU). It's the worker side of the local-file-handle work that already shipped on the reigh-app side.\n\n## Background (why this exists)\n\nreigh-app now has two flows for getting a user's local file (a `FileSystemFileHandle` persisted in the browser) into a worker task as input:\n\n- **Remote worker fallback (already shipped, used today)**: app uploads the bytes to Supabase Storage, puts a public URL into task params, worker downloads from the URL.\n- **Local worker fast path (DORMANT \u2014 what this task enables)**: when the worker is running locally on the same machine as the user's browser, the resolver in `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/{healthcheck,ingest}.ts` is supposed to:\n  1. probe `http://localhost:<port>/health` to detect the worker\n  2. POST file bytes to `http://localhost:<port>/ingest`, receive back `{ path: \"/abs/path/on/disk\" }`\n  3. put `file://<path>` into task params\n  4. after task completion, POST to `http://localhost:<port>/cleanup` with the path so the worker deletes the materialized copy\n\nThe worker today has no HTTP server \u2014 it's a polling client (`source/runtime/worker/server.py` despite its name is not an HTTP server). It also can't read `file://` URLs in most code paths (see #2 below).\n\nThis task is what makes the local fast-path actually work end-to-end.\n\n## What to build\n\n### 1. HTTP server with three endpoints in reigh-worker\n\nAdd a small HTTP server that runs alongside the existing polling worker process. Endpoints:\n\n- `GET /health` \u2014 returns 200 + JSON `{\"ok\": true, \"worker_id\": \"...\", \"version\": \"...\"}`. Cheap, no auth check beyond localhost.\n- `POST /ingest` \u2014 accepts `multipart/form-data` with one file part. Writes the bytes to a known materialization dir (e.g. `~/.reigh-local-files/<uuid>.<ext>`), returns 200 + `{\"path\": \"/abs/path\", \"size\": <bytes>}`. Auth: see below.\n- `POST /cleanup` \u2014 accepts JSON `{\"path\": \"/abs/path\"}`. Validates the path is inside the materialization dir (no escapes), deletes it, returns 204. Auth: see below.\n\n**Auth model**: bind to `127.0.0.1` only (NOT `0.0.0.0`). Even on localhost, an attacker process on the same machine could hit the endpoints \u2014 so require a shared secret on `/ingest` and `/cleanup` via `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header. The token is generated at worker startup, written to a 0600 file at `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (owner-only readable). The reigh-app side reads that file before making requests.\n\n`/health` does NOT require the token (otherwise the probe can't tell \"worker present\" from \"wrong token\"); it leaks zero sensitive info.\n\n**Server framework**: pick whichever is least invasive \u2014 stdlib `http.server` is fine for this scale (<10 req/sec, single user). Avoid pulling in FastAPI/aiohttp unless they're already present in worker deps. Investigate `pyproject.toml` / `requirements.txt` first.\n\n**Lifecycle**: server runs in a background thread spun up at worker startup, dies cleanly on shutdown (SIGINT/SIGTERM). It's PAT-mode-only \u2014 when the worker is launched with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (cloud mode), do NOT start the server. Detection logic exists in `source/runtime/worker/server.py:_resolve_worker_db_client_key()` \u2014 reuse it, don't duplicate.\n\n**Port**: configurable via env var (e.g. `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT`), default to a sensible high port. Write the chosen port to `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` so reigh-app can find it (alongside the auth-token file).\n\n**Materialization dir**: configurable env var (e.g. `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR`), default to `~/.reigh-local-files`. Create with mode 0700 if missing. Hard cap: never write outside this dir; never accept a path component containing `..`.\n\n### 2. file:// support in download_utils.py\n\n`source/utils/download_utils.py` is what the worker uses to fetch task input bytes. Currently it handles `http://` and `https://` URLs by streaming with `requests.get()`. For `file://` URLs (which the new local fast-path produces), it does NOT strip the scheme \u2014 it treats `file:///path/to/foo.jpg` as a literal local path, which fails in most call sites.\n\nFix: in the URL-detection helper (currently around line 205\u2013206), recognize `file://` as a URL scheme. When the scheme is `file`, strip the scheme prefix to get the local path, validate that the path exists and is inside an allowed dir (see materialization dir above \u2014 only allow file:// inputs that point inside that dir), then return the path WITHOUT downloading. Don't copy the file; the worker is allowed to read it in place.\n\nApply this to BOTH `download_image_if_url()` and `download_video_if_url()` (and any siblings). There's apparently a working `file://` handler at `source/media/structure/download.py` \u2014 read it for reference but don't depend on it (the image/video paths are the high-value ones).\n\n### 3. Wire the cleanup hook to actually fire\n\nThe reigh-app side's `complete_task` edge function calls back into the worker's `/cleanup` for each materialized input. Verify that wiring works end-to-end. If the edge function passes the materialized paths through some indirection, make sure the cleanup actually reaches the right worker.\n\nThe edge function code is in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts` (already shipped). Read it to understand the contract \u2014 what URL does it call, what payload does it send, what does it expect back?\n\n## Tests\n\n- `/health` returns 200, no auth.\n- `/ingest` rejects without auth token (401).\n- `/ingest` rejects with wrong token (401).\n- `/ingest` accepts multipart upload, writes file, returns path.\n- `/cleanup` rejects path-traversal (`/etc/passwd`, `../../foo`).\n- `/cleanup` deletes valid file, returns 204.\n- `/cleanup` is idempotent (already-deleted file \u2192 204, not 500).\n- `download_image_if_url` and `download_video_if_url` accept `file://` paths inside materialization dir.\n- Both reject `file://` paths outside materialization dir (security \u2014 don't let task params point at arbitrary local files).\n- Server doesn't start when `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is set (cloud mode).\n- Server starts when only PAT is set (local mode).\n\nUse the worker's existing test framework (likely pytest \u2014 check `pyproject.toml`).\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Don't refactor unrelated code. Don't add new abstractions. Don't add comments explaining the change.\n- Reuse existing helpers \u2014 particularly the auth-mode detection in `source/runtime/worker/server.py`.\n- Don't pull in heavyweight HTTP frameworks. `http.server` from stdlib is sufficient. If you genuinely need something more, justify it in the plan.\n- Keep the server code in a clearly-named module (e.g. `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`).\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- The reigh-app side is already done \u2014 don't touch it unless you find a real bug in `localWorker/{healthcheck,ingest}.ts` that blocks the integration. Flag, don't fix.\n- Don't add metrics, logging frameworks, prometheus, etc. Plain `print()` or whatever the worker already uses is fine.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Run the test suite (`pytest` or whatever the project uses).\n- Manually trace the auth flow end-to-end: server starts \u2192 token + port written \u2192 reigh-app can read both \u2192 `/health` reachable \u2192 `/ingest` accepts an authed upload \u2192 `/cleanup` removes it.\n- Confirm cloud-mode launch (with `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`) does not start the server.\n\n## Deliverables\n\n- New module(s) for the HTTP server, with tests.\n- `download_utils.py` updated for `file://` with tests.\n- Lifecycle integration into worker startup/shutdown.\n- A short note in your final summary describing: which port + dir defaults you chose, where the auth token + port file live on disk, any places the reigh-app side will need a small follow-up tweak.",
    "significant_flags": 10,

        Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

{
  "plan": "# Implementation Plan: Local-worker HTTP endpoints + `file://` input handling\n\n## Overview\n\nGoal: enable the dormant local-worker fast-path. Build a stdlib HTTP server with `/health`, `/ingest`, `/cleanup` (PAT-mode only, bound to `127.0.0.1:8765`, CORS-enabled, Bearer-auth on the two state-changing endpoints); teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` paths inside the materialization dir; and **make cleanup actually fire by doing it worker-side**, not by relying on the hosted edge function.\n\n**Architecture decision driven by iter-2 critique** (resolves FLAG-002/issue_hints-2/scope):\nThe shipped `cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts` is a Deno hosted edge function. It CANNOT reach a user's `localhost:8765`, and the helper it would call (`requestLocalWorkerCleanup` in `src/shared/lib/localWorker/ingest.ts`) is a browser-side module using `import.meta.env`. The brief says \"wire the cleanup hook to actually fire\". With the app side out of scope, the only mechanically-coherent way to honor that is **worker-driven self-cleanup**: when a task finishes, the worker scans the task's input params for `file://` URLs whose paths live in the materialization dir and deletes them itself. The HTTP `/cleanup` endpoint stays — it remains the contract entry-point for any future browser-driven flow — but cleanup no longer depends on it. This keeps the change inside the worker repo (honors \"don't touch app\") AND satisfies the brief's \"wire it to actually fire\" requirement (no longer accepted-debt).\n\n**Other course corrections from iter-2 critique:**\n- **FLAG-005 / correctness** (literal `..` ban): add explicit `..` component check before `resolve()` in both `/cleanup` and `download_utils.py` `file://` validation, matching the brief's exact wording.\n- **issue_hints-1 / all_locations** (auth blocker): keep Bearer auth on `/ingest`+`/cleanup` per the brief's explicit security requirement. The shipped app sends no `Authorization` header today — this is a brief-vs-app product conflict that requires a follow-up app-side change OR a deployer override. To make that follow-up trivial and unblock single-machine smoke testing, add `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` env knob (default off) that, when set, accepts `/ingest`+`/cleanup` without `Authorization`. This is a small, well-scoped escape hatch — not a refactor — and lets the shipped app reach the worker today without the brief's security model being silently dropped.\n\nRepo shape:\n- Polling worker entrypoint: `source/runtime/worker/server.py:main()` (`server.py:248-836`); auth-mode resolver at `server.py:47-64`; per-task success cleanup hook already exists at `server.py:737-738` (`cleanup_generated_files(...)` after `_update_task_complete`).\n- URL fetcher: `source/utils/download_utils.py:_download_file_if_url` (`download_utils.py:202-240`).\n- Reference for `file://` handling: `source/media/structure/download.py:57-64`.\n- Test framework: `pytest` (`pyproject.toml:150-158`); `requests` already a dep.\n\nConstraints baked in: bind `127.0.0.1` only; refuse path-traversal AND literal `..` components; gate startup on PAT-mode; persist `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (0600) and `~/.reigh-local-worker/port`; do NOT pull in heavyweight HTTP frameworks.\n\n## Main Phase\n\n### Step 1: Add the local HTTP server module (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n\n1. **Create** `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py` exposing `start_local_http_server(*, materialization_dir: Path, port: int, worker_id: str, version: str, auth_optional: bool = False) -> LocalHttpServer` and `LocalHttpServer.shutdown()`.\n2. **Implement** with `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer` bound to `(\"127.0.0.1\", port)` plus a `BaseHTTPRequestHandler` subclass.\n3. **Endpoints**:\n   - `GET /health` → `200 application/json` `{\"ok\": true, \"worker_id\": ..., \"version\": ...}`. No auth check.\n   - `POST /ingest` → require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (constant-time compare via `hmac.compare_digest`) UNLESS `auth_optional=True`. Parse `multipart/form-data` with stdlib `cgi.FieldStorage` reading exactly one file part named `file`. Sanitize the upload's filename suffix (regex match the trailing `\\\\.[A-Za-z0-9]+$` only; default `.bin`). Write to `materialization_dir / f\"{uuid4().hex}{suffix}\"` via `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL, 0o600)`. Respond `200` with `{\"path\": \"<abs>\", \"size\": <bytes>}`.\n   - `POST /cleanup` → same auth gate. Read JSON `{\"path\": \"...\"}`. **Validate path components** (addresses FLAG-005 / correctness): if any part of `PurePosixPath(payload_path).parts` (or `Path(payload_path).parts`) equals `\"..\"`, immediately return `400` — matches the brief's literal \"never accept a path component containing `..`\" rule. Then resolve via `Path(path).resolve(strict=False)` and require `is_relative_to(materialization_dir.resolve())`. Outside → `400`. Doesn't exist → `204` (idempotent). Else `unlink()` + `204`.\n   - `OPTIONS <any>` → `204` with CORS headers (see step 4).\n   - Any other path/method → `404`.\n   - Override `log_message` to route through `headless_logger.debug`.\n4. **CORS**: on every response (including `204`/`401`/`400`), set `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, `Access-Control-Allow-Methods: GET, POST, OPTIONS`, `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type`, `Access-Control-Max-Age: 600`. Implement via a `_set_cors_headers(self)` helper called from each `do_*` and `do_OPTIONS`.\n5. **Token generation**: `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` at server construction.\n6. **Persisted state**: ensure `~/.reigh-local-worker/` exists (mode 0700); write `auth-token` with `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC, 0o600)`; write `port` (the actual `server.server_address[1]`) as plain text. On `shutdown()`, best-effort delete both files.\n7. **Materialization dir**: `Path.mkdir(mode=0o700, exist_ok=True, parents=True)`; on POSIX, `os.chmod(0o700)` if pre-existed (best-effort).\n8. **Threading**: `serve_forever()` on a daemon thread; `shutdown()` calls `server.shutdown()`, `server.server_close()`, joins with 5-second timeout.\n\n### Step 2: Wire the server into the worker lifecycle (`source/runtime/worker/server.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Add** a helper near `_resolve_worker_db_client_key` (`server.py:47-64`):\n   ```python\n   def _is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, *, access_token: str | None) -> bool:\n       service_key = os.environ.get(\"SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY\") or os.environ.get(\"SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY\")\n       if service_key:\n           return False\n       if os.environ.get(\"WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE\", \"\").strip().lower() == \"service\":\n           return False\n       return bool(access_token)\n   ```\n2. **Start** the server in `main()` after `_initialize_db_runtime` succeeds (`server.py:500`) and before `task_queue.start()`. Guard with `_is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, access_token=access_token)`.\n3. **Resolve** port: `int(os.environ.get(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT\", \"8765\"))`. Materialization dir: `Path(os.environ.get(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR\", str(Path.home() / \".reigh-local-files\"))).expanduser()`. Auth-optional knob: `os.environ.get(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL\") in (\"1\", \"true\", \"yes\")`.\n4. **Bind** the returned `LocalHttpServer` to a local variable in `main()`, defaulting to `None`. In the existing `finally` block (`server.py:804-835`), guard with `if local_http_server:` and wrap in `try/except BaseException`.\n5. **Bind-failure posture**: if `start_local_http_server` raises `OSError`, log `headless_logger.warning(...)` and continue without the HTTP surface.\n6. **Log** startup once via `headless_logger.essential(f\"Local HTTP server listening on 127.0.0.1:{port} (auth_optional={auth_optional}), materializing to {dir}\")`.\n\n### Step 3: Worker-driven self-cleanup of `file://` task inputs (`source/runtime/worker/server.py`, `source/utils/download_utils.py`)\n**Scope:** Small — addresses FLAG-002 / issue_hints-2 / scope\n\n1. **Add** a small helper `_cleanup_local_materialized_inputs(task_params: dict, materialization_dir: Path) -> int` in `source/utils/download_utils.py` (next to the new `_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir`):\n   - Walk `task_params` recursively (dicts and lists). For each leaf string: if it starts with `file://`, parse the URL, compute the resolved path, and if it `is_relative_to(materialization_dir)` AND `exists()` AND `is_file()`, `unlink(missing_ok=True)` it.\n   - Return the count of files deleted (for logging).\n   - Same `..` component rejection as Step 1's `/cleanup` (so a malicious task params payload can't be coerced into deleting outside the dir).\n2. **Hook it** into the per-task completion path at `source/runtime/worker/server.py:737-738` (next to the existing `cleanup_generated_files` call). Apply ONLY for non-orchestrator task types (`current_task_type not in {\"travel_orchestrator\", \"join_clips_orchestrator\", \"edit_video_orchestrator\"}`) so chained inputs are not deleted mid-chain. Wrap in `try/except BaseException` with `headless_logger.debug_anomaly(...)` on failure — never let cleanup failure mask the task result.\n3. **Also hook** it on the failure path (`server.py:740-757` — the `else` branch and the requeue branch) when the task is being permanently failed (NOT requeued) — at the point `_update_task_complete(..., STATUS_FAILED, ...)` is called. Same orchestrator exclusion applies. This ensures materialized files don't accumulate when tasks fail terminally.\n4. **The `/cleanup` HTTP endpoint stays** as a belt-and-suspenders entry point. If a future browser-side caller (FOLLOW-UP A in Step 7) starts POSTing `/cleanup`, the worker self-cleanup may have already deleted the file — that's fine because `/cleanup` is idempotent (`204` on missing file).\n\n### Step 4: Teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` (`source/utils/download_utils.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Extend** the URL gate at `download_utils.py:205-206`. After `parsed_url = urlparse(...)`:\n   - If `parsed_url.scheme == \"file\"`:\n     - **Reject** if any component of `Path(parsed_url.path).parts` equals `\"..\"` → `DownloadError` (matches Step 1's literal `..` ban; addresses FLAG-005 / correctness).\n     - Resolve with `Path(parsed_url.path).resolve(strict=False)`; require `is_relative_to(_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir().resolve())`, `exists()`, `is_file()`.\n     - On success: return the resolved path string without copying.\n     - On failure: raise `DownloadError(f\"file:// path not allowed: {file_url_or_path}\")`.\n   - Existing `http`/`https` branch is unchanged.\n2. **Add** module-level `_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir() -> Path` returning `Path(os.environ.get(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR\", str(Path.home() / \".reigh-local-files\"))).expanduser()`. Read each call (no cache).\n3. **Coverage**: `download_video_if_url` and `download_image_if_url` both delegate to `_download_file_if_url` (`download_utils.py:248-277`); single change covers both.\n4. **Leave** the `download_target_dir is None` early-return alone; `file://` URLs are handled before that branch.\n\n### Step 5: Tests for the HTTP server (`tests/test_local_http.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n\n1. **Create** `tests/test_local_http.py`. Each test starts a `LocalHttpServer` on a tmp port (probe with `socket` until free) and `materialization_dir=tmp_path / \"mat\"`, then drives `requests` against `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`.\n2. **Cover**:\n   - `test_health_no_auth_returns_ok`.\n   - `test_ingest_missing_auth_returns_401`.\n   - `test_ingest_wrong_token_returns_401`.\n   - `test_ingest_valid_upload_returns_path` — multipart upload, returned `path` exists, `size` matches, file lives under `materialization_dir`, file mode is `0o600`.\n   - `test_ingest_accepts_no_auth_when_auth_optional_true` — start server with `auth_optional=True`, POST without `Authorization` → 200.\n   - `test_cleanup_rejects_dotdot_component_literal` — `{\"path\": \"<mat>/sub/../foo\"}` → 400 even though it would resolve inside (addresses FLAG-005 / correctness).\n   - `test_cleanup_rejects_absolute_path_outside_dir` — `{\"path\": \"/etc/passwd\"}` → 400; verify `Path.unlink` not called via mock.\n   - `test_cleanup_rejects_traversal_escape` — `{\"path\": \"<mat>/../escape\"}` → 400.\n   - `test_cleanup_deletes_valid_file_returns_204`.\n   - `test_cleanup_idempotent_on_missing_file` — second call → 204, not 500.\n   - `test_options_preflight_returns_cors_headers` — `OPTIONS /ingest` with `Origin: http://localhost:5173` → 204 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, `-Methods`, `-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type`.\n   - `test_get_health_includes_cors_headers`.\n   - `test_token_file_mode_is_0600_on_posix` — skip on Windows.\n   - `test_port_file_contains_bound_port`.\n3. **Use** a pytest fixture to start/stop the server cleanly per test; assert background thread exits on `shutdown()`.\n\n### Step 6: Tests for `file://` handling, lifecycle gating, and self-cleanup\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Create** `tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py`:\n   - `test_download_image_accepts_file_url_inside_materialization_dir`: real fake file under `tmp_path/\"mat\"`; `monkeypatch.setenv(\"REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR\", str(tmp_path/\"mat\"))`; call `download_image_if_url(f\"file://{path}\", download_target_dir=tmp_path/\"out\")`; returned string equals resolved path; file NOT copied to `out`.\n   - Same shape for `download_video_if_url`.\n   - `test_rejects_file_url_outside_materialization_dir` → `DownloadError`.\n   - `test_rejects_file_url_with_literal_dotdot_component` — even when the resolved path falls back inside (addresses FLAG-005 / correctness).\n   - `test_rejects_file_url_with_traversal_escape` — `file:///etc/passwd` → `DownloadError`.\n   - `test_file_url_works_when_download_target_dir_is_none`.\n2. **Create** `tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py`:\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_true_with_pat_only` (clear service envs, `WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE` empty, `access_token=\"pat-token\"`) → `True`.\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_service_role_key_set` → `False`.\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_service_key_alias_set` → `False`.\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_with_anon_key_only` → `False`.\n   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_auth_mode_service` → `False`.\n   - Use `types.SimpleNamespace()` for `cli_args`.\n3. **Create** `tests/test_self_cleanup.py` (addresses FLAG-002 / issue_hints-2 / scope):\n   - `test_cleanup_local_materialized_inputs_walks_nested_params` — task params with `file://` URLs nested inside dicts and lists; helper deletes all of them and returns the count.\n   - `test_cleanup_local_materialized_inputs_skips_files_outside_dir` — `file://` URL pointing outside materialization dir is left untouched.\n   - `test_cleanup_local_materialized_inputs_skips_http_urls` — `https://...` strings are not even examined as paths.\n   - `test_cleanup_local_materialized_inputs_rejects_dotdot_component` — `file://<mat>/sub/../foo` is NOT deleted (matches `/cleanup` semantics).\n   - `test_cleanup_local_materialized_inputs_idempotent_on_missing_file` — file already gone; returns 0 without raising.\n\n### Step 7: Document the integration gaps and the auth-optional escape hatch (no app-side code change)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **In the deliverable summary**, enumerate:\n   - **FOLLOW-UP A (auth)**: shipped `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/ingest.ts` does not send `Authorization`. Two ways to unblock today:\n     - **Option 1 (preferred long-term)**: app reads `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` and sends `Authorization: Bearer <token>` on `/ingest` + future `/cleanup` calls.\n     - **Option 2 (immediate single-machine smoke)**: deployer sets `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` on the worker. The brief's security requirement is then opt-out for the deployer, not silently dropped. Document this as a dev-only knob; production worker setups should leave it unset.\n   - **FOLLOW-UP B (cleanup)** — **demoted to non-blocking**: the worker now self-cleans `file://` inputs after each non-orchestrator task (see Step 3), so accumulated files are no longer a blocker. The HTTP `/cleanup` endpoint is dormant unless someone wires a browser-side or worker-orchestrator caller in future. The mechanically-incoherent v2 \"edge function calls requestLocalWorkerCleanup\" suggestion is withdrawn — `cleanupMaterializedInputs.ts` is a hosted Deno function and cannot reach `localhost:8765`; it should keep just logging `kind:file` records (or be deleted in a future app-side cleanup task).\n   - **FOLLOW-UP C (port discovery, optional)**: app could read `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` for non-default ports. Worker writes it; app currently uses the hardcoded `8765` default and we match it.\n2. **Do not edit** the app side — it is explicitly out of scope per the brief.\n\n### Step 8: Run the test suite\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Run** `pytest tests/test_local_http.py tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py tests/test_self_cleanup.py -v` first.\n2. **Run** the broader `pytest -x` to confirm no regressions. Confirm the new module has no heavy import side effects.\n\n## Execution Order\n\n1. Land Step 1 (server module) and Step 5 (server tests) together — module is developable in isolation.\n2. Land Step 4 (`file://` support in download_utils) and Step 6's `test_download_utils_file_url.py` together.\n3. Land Step 3 (worker self-cleanup helper + hook) and Step 6's `test_self_cleanup.py` together.\n4. Land Step 2 (lifecycle wiring) and Step 6's lifecycle tests last — they touch the long-running worker entrypoint.\n5. Step 7 is writeup-only alongside the final summary.\n\n## Validation Order\n\n1. Targeted unit tests from Steps 5 + 6 first (cheapest signal, no GPU/wgp imports).\n2. Full `pytest` last to confirm no breakage in adjacent suites.\n3. Manual smoke (PAT-mode worker on port 8765, with `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` set so the shipped app can talk to it today): `curl /health` (200, CORS headers), OPTIONS preflight (204), `curl -F file=@... /ingest` (200), submit a task with `file://...` in params, watch the file disappear after task completion (worker self-cleanup). Plan revised with one substantive architecture change and two targeted fixes:

**Architecture pivot (resolves the stuck cleanup-mechanics group):** Cleanup is now **worker-driven self-cleanup** — after each non-orchestrator task completes (success or terminal failure), the worker scans its own task params for `file://` URLs in the materialization dir and unlinks them. The HTTP `/cleanup` endpoint stays as a contract entry-point but isn't depended on for correctness. This satisfies the brief's "wire the cleanup hook to actually fire" while keeping the change inside the worker repo.

**FLAG-005/correctness:** Added literal `..` component check before `resolve()` at all three path-validation sites (HTTP `/cleanup`, `download_utils.py` `file://`, self-cleanup helper) with matching tests.

**issue_hints-1/all_locations (auth):** Added `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL` env knob (default off) so deployers can match the shipped app's no-Authorization behavior on a single machine. Brief's security requirement stays the default; the override is explicit and documented.
